\displaystyle\bigl(a^{\prime}N^{2/3},pa^{\prime}N^{2/3}-(R+K)N^{1/3}\bigr)\quad\text{to}\quad\bigl(b^{\prime}N^{2/3},pb^{\prime}N^{2/3}-(R+K)N^{1/3}\bigr)

conditioned to remain strictly below the line
px-(R+K)N^{1/3}
on
[a^{\prime}N^{2/3}+1,b^{\prime}N^{2/3}-1]
. Let
\overline{B}(x):=B(x)-px
.
By Lemma
9.7
, conditional on the
\sigma
-algebra
\mathcal{F}:=\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{ext}}(\{k\},\llbracket a^{\prime}N^{2/3},b^{\prime}N^{2/3}\rrbracket,\bm{L}^{N})
, the law of
L^{N}_{k}|_{[a^{\prime}N^{2/3},b^{\prime}N^{2/3}]}
is that of
B
reweighted by the Radon-Nikodym derivative proportional to
W(B,L^{N}_{k-1},L^{N}_{k+1})=W_{\mathrm{up}}(B,L^{N}_{k-1})W_{\mathrm{low}}(B,L^{N}_{k+1})
(recall from Definition
3.15
), where we drop the interval
\llbracket a^{\prime}N^{2/3},b^{\prime}N^{2/3}\rrbracket
on which
W
is evaluated from the notation.
Then, by the Hall-Littlewood Gibbs property, the definitions of
a^{\prime}
and
b^{\prime}
, and since
\smash{W_{\mathrm{up}}(B,L^{N}_{k-1})\leq 1}
, with
Z=\mathbb E_{\mathcal{F}}[W(B,L^{N}_{k-1},L^{N}_{k+1})]
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\mathsf{E}(t,a,b)\ \Big|\ \inf_{xN^{-2/3}\in[a,b]}\overline{L}^{N}_{k-1}(x)>-(R-\delta)N^{1/3}\right)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbb E_{\mathcal{F}}\!\left[\mathbbm{1}_{\overline{B}(tN^{2/3})\leq-(R+2K)N^{1/3}}W(B,L^{N}_{k-1},L^{N}_{k+1})Z^{-1}\right]\!\!\ \Big|\ \!\inf_{xN^{-2/3}\in[a,b]}\overline{L}^{N}_{k-1}(x)>-(R-\delta)N^{1/3}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbb E_{\mathcal{F}}\left[\mathbbm{1}_{\overline{B}(tN^{2/3})\leq-(R+2K)N^{1/3}}W_{\mathrm{low}}(B,L^{N}_{k+1})Z^{-1}\right]\ \Big|\ \inf_{xN^{-2/3}\in[a,b]}\overline{L}^{N}_{k-1}(x)>-(R-\delta)N^{1/3}\right].

Since
\overline{L}^{N}_{k-1}\geq-(R-\delta)N^{1/3}
throughout the interval
[aN^{2/3},bN^{2/3}]
and
\overline{B}\leq-(R+K)N^{1/3}
on the interval
[a^{\prime}N^{2/3},b^{\prime}N^{2/3}]
, it follows that
W_{\mathrm{up}}(B,L^{N}_{k-1})\geq(1-q^{(\delta+K)N^{1/3}})^{(b-a)N^{2/3}}\geq\frac{1}{2}
for all
N>N_{0}
, for an
N_{0}
which can be taken to be increasing in
b-a
, decreasing in
\delta
, and independent of
K
as well as
k
. Thus
Z\geq\frac{1}{2}\mathbb E_{\mathcal{F}}[W_{\mathrm{low}}(B,L^{N}_{k+1})]
, which implies that the previous display is upper bounded by

\displaystyle 2\cdot\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\mathbb E_{\mathcal{F}}\left[\mathbbm{1}_{\overline{B}(tN^{2/3})\leq-(R+2K)N^{1/3}}W_{\mathrm{low}}(B,L^{N}_{k+1})\right]}{\mathbb E_{\mathcal{F}}\left[W_{\mathrm{low}}(B,L^{N}_{k+1})\right]}\ \bigg|\ \inf_{xN^{-2/3}\in[a,b]}\overline{L}^{N}_{k-1}(x)>-(R-\delta)N^{1/3}\right]

\displaystyle=2\cdot\mathbb{E}\Biggl[\frac{\mathbb E_{\mathcal{F}}\left[W_{\mathrm{low}}(B,L^{N}_{k+1})\mid\overline{B}(tN^{2/3})\leq-(R+2K)N^{1/3}\right]}{\mathbb E_{\mathcal{F}}\left[W_{\mathrm{low}}(B,L^{N}_{k+1})\right]}

\displaystyle\qquad\times\mathbb P_{\mathcal{F}}\left(\overline{B}(tN^{2/3})\leq-(R+2K)N^{1/3}\right)\ \bigg|\ \inf_{xN^{-2/3}\in[a,b]}\overline{L}^{N}_{k-1}(x)>-(R-\delta)N^{1/3}\Biggr].

By weak monotonicity (Corollary
8.5
) and noting that the conditional probability factor is deterministic, the previous display is upper bounded by

\displaystyle 2c(q)^{-1}\cdot\mathbb{P}\left(\overline{B}(tN^{2/3})\leq-(R+2K)N^{1/3}\right).

This probability is in turn upper bounded by
C\exp(-cK^{2}/(b^{\prime}-a^{\prime}))\leq C\exp(-cK^{2}/(b-a))
. This follows by stochastic monotonicity of Bernoulli random walk bridges (Lemma
8.2
):
B(tN^{2/3})\geq B^{\prime}(tN^{2/3})
where
B^{\prime}
is a Bernoulli random walk bridge from
(a^{\prime}N^{2/3},pa^{\prime}N^{2/3}-(R+\frac{3}{2}K)N^{1/3})
to
(b^{\prime}N^{2/3},pb^{\prime}N^{2/3}-(R+\frac{3}{2}K)N^{1/3})
(i.e., lowered endpoints) conditioned on staying below
px-(R+K)N^{1/3}
, so we only need to bound

\mathbb{P}\left(\tilde{B}(tN^{2/3})\leq-(R+2K)N^{1/3}\ \Big|\ \sup_{xN^{-2/3}\in[a^{\prime},b^{\prime}]}\tilde{B}(x)-px\leq-(R+K)N^{1/3}\right),

where
\tilde{B}
is a Bernoulli random walk bridge with the same endpoints as
B^{\prime}
. There exists
\eta=\eta(b-a)>0
such that the probability of the event being conditioned on is at least
\eta
, and
\eta
is decreasing in
b-a
(the fact that
\eta
can be taken independent of
K
uses that
K\geq 1
); in particular, the bound is uniform in
N
. The probability of
\tilde{B}(tN^{2/3})\leq-(R+2K)N^{1/3}
is at most
\tilde{C}\exp(-cK^{2}/(b-a))
by Lemma
6.13
. This completes the proof with
C=\tilde{C}\eta^{-1}
.
∎

10.
Uniform separation of curves

In this section we prove Proposition
9.4
which, recall, assumes control on the lower tail of the
k
th
curve and on separation between the first
k-1
curves and obtains control on the separation between the first
k
curves. In this section we again assume
\bm{L}^{N}
satisfies Assumptions
1
and
2
without further explicit mention.

10.1.
Control on the lower curve

We will need some control over the
k
th
curve, in particular that it does not increase too quickly, at least at some random location. For this we prove the following deterministic statement about continuous functions whose range is controlled.

Lemma 10.1
.

Let
T>0
,
\delta\in(0,\frac{1}{30}T)
, and
f:[0,T]\to\mathbb{R}
be continuous. Suppose
M>0
is such that
\sup_{x,y\in[0,T]}|f(x)-f(y)|\leq M
. Then, with
P=3MT^{-1}
, there exists
U\in[0,\frac{1}{2}T]
such that

\displaystyle f(x)\leq f(U)+P(x-U)\text{ for }x\in[U,U+\delta].

Proof.

Let
f^{-}(x)=f(x)-Px
.
Next for
i=0,1,\ldots
, let
I_{i}=[i\cdot 2\delta,(i+1)2\delta]
,
J_{i}=I_{i}+\delta=[(i+\frac{1}{2})2\delta,(i+\frac{3}{2})2\delta]
,
x^{I}_{i}=\argmax_{I_{i}}f^{-}
and
x^{J}_{i}=\argmax_{J_{i}}f^{-}
(taking the smallest in the case of multiple maximizers).

We claim that there is an
i
such that
I_{i}\subseteq[0,\frac{1}{2}T]
,
J_{i}\subseteq[0,\frac{1}{2}T]
, and at least one of
x_{i}^{I}\in[\inf I_{i},\inf I_{i}+\delta]
or
x_{i}^{J}\in[\inf J_{i},\inf J_{i}+\delta]
holds, i.e., the maximizer lies in the left half of the interval. Observe that this would complete the proof, since the length of the second half of
I_{i}
or
J_{i}
is
\delta
, and for such an
i
, by taking
U=x^{I}_{i}
(if it is the
I_{i}
case), it holds for
x\in[U,\sup I_{i}]
that
f(x)-Px\leq f(x^{I}_{i})-P(x^{I}_{i})
, and similarly for the
J_{i}
case.

Suppose our claim does not hold. Let
i_{\max}
be the largest
i
such that
J_{i}\subseteq[0,\frac{1}{2}T]
. Under our contrapositive hypothesis,
x^{J}_{i}\in[(i+1)2\delta,(i+\frac{3}{2})2\delta]
and
x^{I}_{i}\in[(i+\frac{1}{2})2\delta,(i+1)2\delta]
for each
i\in\llbracket 0,i_{\max}\rrbracket
.
This implies that
x^{I}_{i}\in J_{i}
and
x^{J}_{i}\in I_{i+1}
for each
i
, so for each
i
,

\displaystyle f^{-}(x^{J}_{i})\geq f^{-}(x^{I}_{i})\geq f^{-}(x^{J}_{i-1}).

Iterating, this gives that

\displaystyle f^{-}(x^{J}_{i_{\max}})\geq f^{-}(x^{J}_{0})\implies f(x^{J}_{i_{\max}})\geq f(x^{J}_{0})+P(x^{J}_{i_{\max}}-x^{J}_{0}).

This contradicts the fact that
\sup_{x,y\in[0,T]}|f(x)-f(y)|\leq M
since
x^{J}_{i_{\max}}\geq\frac{1}{2}T-2\delta
,
x^{J}_{1}\leq 3\delta
, and
P=3M/T
(we implicitly use that
5\delta\leq\frac{1}{6}T
). This completes the proof.
∎

To have control on the range of
L^{N}_{k}
as needed to apply Lemma
10.1
, we need control on the upper tail of
\smash{L^{N}_{k}}
over an interval in addition to the lower tail control we have assumed. This uniform upper tail control is provided by the following bound due to
[
CD18
]
on the upper tail of
L^{N}_{1}
, and the fact that the curves in
\bm{L}^{N}
are ordered. Recall the notation
\smash{\overline{L}^{N}_{j}(x)=L^{N}_{j}(x)-px}
.

Proposition 10.2

(
[
CD18
, Lemma 5.2]
)
.

Let
\varepsilon>0
and
T>0
. There exist
N_{0}=N_{0}(\varepsilon,T)
and
M=M(\varepsilon,T)
such that, for
N\geq N_{0}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\sup_{sN^{-2/3}\in[-T,T]}\overline{L}^{N}_{1}(s)\geq MN^{1/3}\right)\leq\varepsilon.

The following statement uses Lemma
10.1
to obtain separation between
L^{N}_{k-1}
and
L^{N}_{k}
at a single random point; see Figure
18
. It will be proved in Section
10.2
.

Figure 18
.
A depiction of the setup for Lemma
10.3
. The dashed slanted lines represent the lines of slope
\pm PN^{-1/3}
upper bounding
\smash{\overline{L}^{N}_{k}}
on two intervals of size
N^{2/3}
. Uniform separation between the blue curves holds with high probability by assumption, and the lemma yields random points
U_{1}
and
U_{2}
where separation between
\smash{\overline{L}^{N}_{k-1}}
and
\smash{\overline{L}^{N}_{k}}
holds.

Lemma 10.3

(One-point separation)
.

Fix
k\in\mathbb{N}
and
T\geq 2
, and let
\mathsf{Sep}^{N}_{k-2}(\eta,[-3T,-T]):=

\mathsf{Sep}^{N}_{k-2}(\eta,[-3T,-T],\bm{L}^{N})
be as in (
9.1
) and similarly for
\mathsf{Sep}^{N}_{k-2}(\eta,[T,3T])
.
Suppose for any
\varepsilon>0
there exist
M=M(T,k,\varepsilon)
and
N_{0}=N_{0}(k,T,\varepsilon)
such that, for
N\geq N_{0}
,

(10.1)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\inf_{xN^{-2/3}\in[-2T,2T]}\overline{L}^{N}_{k}(x)\geq-MN^{1/3}\right)\geq 1-\varepsilon

and there exists
\eta=\eta(T,k,\varepsilon)>0
such that, for
N\geq N_{0}
,

(10.2)

\displaystyle\min\Bigl\{\mathbb{P}\left(\mathsf{Sep}^{N}_{k-2}(\eta,[-3T,-T])\right),\mathbb{P}\left(\mathsf{Sep}^{N}_{k-2}(\eta,[T,3T])\right)\Bigr\}\geq 1-\varepsilon.

Now fix any
\varepsilon>0
. There exists
P=P(k,T,\varepsilon)
,
\delta=\delta(k,T,\varepsilon)>0
, and
N_{0}=N_{0}(k,T,\varepsilon)
such that the following holds for
N>N_{0}
. With probability at least
1-\varepsilon
, there exist random
L^{N}_{k}
-measurable points
U_{1}\in\left[-2T,-\frac{3}{2}T\right]
and
U_{2}\in[\frac{3}{2}T,2T]
such that
L^{N}_{k-1}(U_{i}N^{2/3})>L^{N}_{k}(U_{i}N^{2/3})+\delta N^{1/3}
for
i=1,2
, and

\displaystyle\overline{L}^{N}_{k}(x)

\displaystyle\leq\overline{L}^{N}_{k}(U_{1}N^{2/3})+(x-U_{1}N^{2/3})PN^{-1/3}\quad\text{for}\quad xN^{-2/3}\in[U_{1},U_{1}+1],

\displaystyle\overline{L}^{N}_{k}(x)

\displaystyle\leq\overline{L}^{N}_{k}(U_{2}N^{2/3})+(U_{2}N^{2/3}-x)PN^{-1/3}\quad\text{for}\quad xN^{-2/3}\in[U_{2}-1,U_{2}].

Note that the range of
L^{N}_{k}
is of order
N^{1/3}
and the interval size is of order
N^{2/3}
, so that the quantity
3MT^{-1}
in Lemma
10.1
is of order
N^{-1/3}
; this is the source of the
N^{-1/3}
factors in the previous display.

Lemma
10.3
is the main step in the proof of Proposition
9.4
on uniform separation between these curves. A second technical ingredient that will be needed is an estimate about uniform separation for a Bernoulli random walk bridge, which we state next. As its proof is fairly straightforward, it will be proved in Appendix
B
.

Lemma 10.4

(Random walk uniform separation)
.

Fix
M
,
p
, and
T\geq 2
. Let
U_{1}\in[-2T,-\smash{\frac{3}{2}}T]
and
U_{2}\in[\smash{\frac{3}{2}}T,2T]
. Let
B^{\mathrm{RW}}
be a Bernoulli random walk bridge from
(U_{1}N^{2/3},z_{1})
to
(U_{2}N^{2/3},z_{2})
, for some
z_{i}\in\mathbb{Z}
such that
z_{i}-pU_{i}N^{2/3}\in[-MN^{1/3},MN^{1/3}]
for
i\in\{1,2\}
;

B^{\mathrm{Br}}
be a Brownian bridge of variance
p(1-p)
with the same endpoints; and
f:[U_{1}N^{2/3},U_{2}N^{2/3}]\to\mathbb{R}
be a function with
f(U_{i}N^{2/3})\leq z_{i}
for
i\in\{1,2\}
. Let
\{B^{\mathrm{Br}}\geq f\}
denote
\{B^{\mathrm{Br}}(x)\geq f(x)\ \forall x\in[U_{1}N^{2/3},U_{2}N^{2/3}]\}
(and analogously for
\{B^{\mathrm{RW}}\geq f\}
) and suppose, for some
\omega,\rho>0
, that

\mathbb{P}\left(B^{\mathrm{Br}}\geq f+\omega N^{1/3}\right)\geq\rho.

Then for any
\varepsilon>0
there exists
\delta=\delta(\varepsilon,\rho,M,p,\omega,T)>0
such that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\inf_{x\in[-TN^{2/3},TN^{2/3}]}\left(B^{\mathrm{RW}}(x)-f(x)\right)\leq\delta N^{1/3}\ \Big|\ B^{\mathrm{RW}}\geq f\right)\leq\varepsilon.

We give the proof of Proposition
9.4
assuming the previous two statements now. The proof will involve an event
\mathsf{Fav}
consisting of favorable conditions on
\bm{L}^{N}
. We point out that a different event with the same name was used in the proof of Corollary
6.15
, and in fact we will use the same name for a number of different events in upcoming proofs. The name will refer to a single event locally within each proof, however, and so should not cause confusion.

Proof of Proposition
9.4
.

By Lemma
10.3
, using (
9.3
), there exists
P=P(\varepsilon,T,k)
and
\eta=\eta(\varepsilon,T,k)>0
such that with probability at least
1-\varepsilon
there exist random
L^{N}_{k}
-measurable
U_{1}\in[-2T,-\frac{3T}{2}]
and
U_{2}\in[\frac{3}{2}T,2T]
such that
L^{N}_{k-1}(U_{i}N^{2/3})>L^{N}_{k}(U_{i}N^{2/3})+\eta N^{1/3}
for
i=1,2
and

(10.3)

\begin{split}\overline{L}^{N}_{k}(x)&\leq\overline{L}^{N}_{k}(U_{1}N^{2/3})+(x-U_{1}N^{2/3})PN^{-1/3}\quad\text{for}\quad xN^{-2/3}\in[U_{1},U_{1}+1],\quad\text{and}\\
\overline{L}^{N}_{k}(x)&\leq\overline{L}^{N}_{k}(U_{2}N^{2/3})+(U_{2}N^{2/3}-x)PN^{-1/3}\quad\text{for}\quad xN^{-2/3}\in[U_{2}-1,U_{2}].\end{split}

By (
9.3
), after decreasing
\eta>0
if necessary (and dropping
\bm{L}^{N}
from the notation of
\mathsf{Sep}^{N}_{k-2}
), we have that

(10.4)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\mathsf{Sep}^{N}_{k-2}(\eta,[-2T,2T])^{c}\right)\leq\varepsilon.

Let
\delta\in(0,\eta)
be a real number, to be set precisely later, but for now we will use that
\delta<\eta
. It clearly holds using (
10.4
) that, since
\delta<\eta
,

(10.5)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\mathsf{Sep}^{N}_{k-1}(\delta,[-T,T])^{c}\right)\leq\mathbb{P}\left(\inf_{xN^{-2/3}\in[-T,T]}\left(L^{N}_{k-1}(x)-L^{N}_{k}(x)\right)\leq\delta N^{1/3}\right)+\varepsilon.

Let
\mathcal{F}=\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{ext}}(k-1,\llbracket U_{1}N^{2/3},U_{2}N^{2/3}\rrbracket)
be as in Definition
3.14
, i.e., the
\sigma
-algebra generated by
\{L^{N}_{i}(x):(i,x)\not\in\llbracket 1,k-1\rrbracket\times\llbracket U_{1}N^{2/3}+1,U_{2}N^{2/3}-1\rrbracket\}
. Recall that
U_{i}
are
L^{N}_{k}
-measurable. Define the
\mathcal{F}
-measurable favourable event
\mathsf{Fav}
by

\displaystyle\mathsf{Fav}

\displaystyle:=\left\{\min_{i=1,\ldots,k-1}\min_{xN^{-2/3}\in\{U_{1},U_{2}\}}\left(L^{N}_{i}(x)-L^{N}_{i+1}(x)\right)>\eta N^{1/3}\right\}

\displaystyle\qquad\cap\left\{\sup_{xN^{-2/3}\in[-2T,2T]}|\overline{L}^{N}_{k}(x)|\leq MN^{1/3}\right\}.

By the definition of
U_{1}
and
U_{2}
and (
10.4
), the upper bound on the supremum (Proposition
10.2
), and the upper bound on the lower tail (
9.3
), there exists
M=M(\varepsilon,k,T)
such that
\mathbb{P}(\mathsf{Fav}^{c})\leq 3\varepsilon
. Let
\bm{B}=(B_{1},\ldots,B_{k-1})
be a
(k-1)
-tuple of independent Bernoulli random walk bridges, with
B_{i}
from
(U_{1}N^{2/3},L^{N}_{i}(U_{1}N^{2/3}))
to
(U_{2}N^{2/3},L^{N}_{i}(U_{2}N^{2/3}))
. Now
[U_{1}N^{2/3},U_{2}N^{2/3}]
forms a stopping domain (Definition
6.1
) with respect to
(L^{N}_{1},\ldots,L^{N}_{k-1})
; by the strong Gibbs property (Proposition
6.2
) and the just recorded bound on
\mathbb{P}(\mathsf{Fav}^{c})
, the first term on the righthand side of (
10.5
) is upper bounded by (with
W
as in (
3.9
))

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\mathbb E_{\mathcal{F}}\left[\mathbbm{1}_{\inf_{xN^{-2/3}\in[-T,T]}(B_{k-1}(x)-L^{N}_{k}(x))\leq\delta N^{1/3}}W(\bm{B},L^{N}_{k})\right]}{\mathbb E_{\mathcal{F}}[W(\bm{B},L^{N}_{k})]}\mathbbm{1}_{\mathsf{Fav}}\right]+4\varepsilon

(10.6)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\mathbb E_{\mathcal{F}}\left[\mathbbm{1}_{\inf_{xN^{-2/3}\in[-T,T]}(B_{k-1}(x)-L^{N}_{k}(x))\leq\delta N^{1/3}}W(\bm{B},L^{N}_{k})\ \bigl|\ B_{k-1}\geq L^{N}_{k}\right]}{\mathbb E_{\mathcal{F}}[W(\bm{B},L^{N}_{k})]}\mathbbm{1}_{\mathsf{Fav}}\right]+4\varepsilon,

where
B_{k-1}\geq L^{N}_{k}
is shorthand for
B_{k-1}(x)\geq L^{N}_{k}(x)
for all
xN^{-2/3}\in[U_{1},U_{2}]
and the inequality uses that
W(\bm{B},L^{N}_{k})=0
if
B_{k-1}(x)<L^{N}_{k}(x)
for any
xN^{-2/3}\in[U_{1},U_{2}]
.

Figure 19
.
The event we consider (after subtracting off the affine shift of slope
p
) to obtain a lower bound on the denominator in (
10.6
). The dashed lines are the lines of slope
\pm PN^{-1/3}
that upper bound
L^{N}_{k}
on
[U_{i}N^{1/3},(U_{i}+1)N^{2/3}]
for
i=1,2
respectively, and the separation between the points in black at
U_{i}N^{2/3}
is at least
\eta N^{1/3}
, implying that the separation between the resampled curves is at least
\frac{1}{3}\eta N^{1/3}
throughout
[U_{1}N^{2/3},U_{2}N^{2/3}]
.

We first lower bound the denominator of the first term of (
10.6
). We construct deterministic disjoint channels of width
\smash{\frac{2}{3}}\eta N^{1/3}
which are separated by
\smash{\frac{1}{3}}\eta N^{1/3}
for the paths to stay inside; see Figure
19
. Let
Y=\max(MN^{1/3},\smash{\overline{L}^{N}_{k}(U_{1}N^{2/3})}+PN^{1/3},\smash{\overline{L}^{N}_{k}(U_{2}N^{2/3})}+PN^{1/3})
. Let
\smash{\ell^{\mathrm{L}}_{i}}:[U_{1}N^{2/3},(U_{1}+1)N^{2/3}]
be the line joining

\left(U_{1}N^{2/3},\overline{L}^{N}_{i}(U_{1}N^{2/3})-\tfrac{1}{3}\eta N^{1/3}\right)\quad\text{and}\quad\left((U_{1}+1)N^{2/3},Y+(k-i)\eta N^{1/3}\right).

Similarly let
\ell^{\mathrm{R}}_{i}:[(U_{2}-1)N^{2/3},U_{2}N^{2/3}]
be the line joining

\left(U_{2}N^{2/3},\overline{L}^{N}_{i}(U_{2}N^{2/3})-\tfrac{1}{3}\eta N^{1/3}\right)\quad\text{and}\quad\left((U_{2}-1)N^{2/3},Y+(k-i)\eta N^{1/3}\right).

Note that, on
\mathsf{Fav}
, it holds that

(10.7)

\displaystyle\ell^{*}_{i}(x)-\ell^{*}_{i+1}(x)\geq\eta N^{1/3}

for each
*\in\{\mathrm{L},\mathrm{R}\}
and
xN^{-2/3}\in[U_{1},U_{1}+1]
and
[U_{2}-1,U_{2}]
respectively, by the fact that
L^{N}_{i}(U_{j}N^{2/3})-L^{N}_{i+1}(U_{j}N^{2/3})\geq\eta N^{1/3}
for each
j\in\{1,2\}
on
\mathsf{Fav}
. For
x\smash{N^{-2/3}}\in[U_{1},U_{2}]
and
i\in\llbracket 1,k-1\rrbracket
, define the set
\mathrm{Chan}_{i}(x)\subseteq\mathbb{R}
(short for channels) by

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\left[(Y+(k-i)\eta)N^{1/3},(Y+(k-i+\tfrac{2}{3})\eta)N^{1/3}\right],&\text{if }\ xN^{-2/3}\in[U_{1}+1,U_{2}-1]\\
\left[\ell^{\mathrm{L}}_{i}(x),\ell^{\mathrm{L}}_{i}(x)+\tfrac{2}{3}\eta N^{1/3}\right],&\text{if }\ xN^{-2/3}\in[U_{1},U_{1}+1]\\
\left[\ell^{\mathrm{R}}_{i}(x),\ell^{\mathrm{R}}_{i}(x)+\tfrac{2}{3}\eta N^{1/3}\right],&\text{if }\ xN^{-2/3}\in[U_{2}-1,U_{2}].\end{cases}

Note, by (
10.7
) and the first line of the above definition of
\mathrm{Chan}_{i}(x)
, that for each
x\smash{N^{-2/3}}\in[U_{1},U_{2}]
and
i\in\llbracket 1,k-1\rrbracket
,
\mathrm{Chan}_{i}(x)
is an interval of size
\frac{2}{3}\eta N^{1/3}
and

\inf\mathrm{Chan}_{i}(x)\geq\sup\mathrm{Chan}_{i+1}(x)+\smash{\frac{1}{3}}\eta N^{1/3}

(the infimum and supremum being of the set for fixed
x
). Also observe that
\inf\mathrm{Chan}_{k-1}(x)\geq\overline{L}^{N}_{k}(U_{1}N^{2/3})+PN^{-1/3}(x-U_{1}N^{2/3})+\frac{1}{3}\eta N^{1/3}
for all
x\in[U_{1}N^{2/3},(U_{1}+1)N^{2/3}]
and
\inf\mathrm{Chan}_{k-1}(x)\geq\overline{L}^{N}_{k}(U_{2}N^{2/3})+PN^{-1/3}(U_{2}N^{2/3}-x)+\frac{1}{3}\eta N^{1/3}
for all
x\in[(U_{2}-1)N^{2/3},U_{2}N^{2/3}]
.

Let
\overline{B}_{i}(x)=B_{i}(x)-px
and define the event
\mathsf{Chan}
of staying in the channels by

\mathsf{Chan}:=\left\{\overline{B}_{i}(x)\in\mathrm{Chan}_{i}(x)\text{ for all }x\in[U_{1}N^{2/3},U_{2}N^{2/3}],i\in\llbracket 1,k-1\rrbracket\right\}.

Then observe
\mathbb{P}(\mathsf{Chan})
is lower bounded by some
\alpha=\alpha(\eta,k,T)=\alpha(\varepsilon,k,T)>0
(as can be seen using the weak convergence of
\overline{\bm{B}}
to independent Brownian bridges whose endpoints are separated by at least
\eta
, after rescaling); this also uses that
\min_{i\in\llbracket 1,k-1\rrbracket,x\in\{U_{1},U_{2}\}}\overline{L}^{N}_{i}(xN^{2/3})\geq-MN^{1/3}
holds on
\mathsf{Fav}
(since
L^{N}_{i}
are ordered). Since
\smash{\overline{L}^{N}_{k}}(x)\leq MN^{1/3}
for
xN^{-2/3}\in[-2T,2T]
on
\mathsf{Fav}
and the channels are separated from each other and from
\smash{\overline{L}^{N}_{k}}
by at least
\smash{\frac{1}{3}\eta N^{1/3}}
, it follows that, on
\mathsf{Chan}
, by Lemma
9.1
,

\displaystyle W(\bm{B},L^{N}_{k})\geq(1-q^{\frac{1}{3}\eta N^{1/3}})^{4kTN^{2/3}}\geq\tfrac{1}{2}

for all large enough
N
. Thus we obtain that, on
\mathsf{Fav}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb E_{\mathcal{F}}[W(\bm{B},L^{N}_{k})]\geq\mathbb E_{\mathcal{F}}[W(\bm{B},L^{N}_{k})\mid\mathsf{Chan}]\cdot\mathbb P_{\mathcal{F}}(\mathsf{Chan})\geq\tfrac{1}{2}\alpha.

Putting this back into (
10.6
) and using
W(\bm{B},L^{N}_{k})\leq 1
yields that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\inf_{xN^{-2/3}\in[-T,T]}\left(L^{N}_{k-1}(x)-L^{N}_{k}(x)\right)\leq\delta N^{1/3}\right)

\displaystyle\leq 2\alpha^{-1}\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbb P_{\mathcal{F}}\left(\inf_{xN^{-2/3}\in[-T,T]}\left(B_{k-1}(x)-L^{N}_{k}(x)\right)\leq\delta N^{1/3}\ \Big|\ B_{k-1}\geq L^{N}_{k}\right)\mathbbm{1}_{\mathsf{Fav}}\right]+4\varepsilon.

By Lemma
10.4
, there exists
\delta=\delta(M,T,\varepsilon,k,\alpha)=\delta(T,\varepsilon,k)>0
such that the conditional probability in the previous display is upper bounded by
\alpha\varepsilon
; the hypothesis on the lower bound on the probability of
\{B^{\mathrm{Br}}(x)\geq L^{N}_{k}(x)+\omega N^{1/3}\ \forall x\in[U_{1},U_{2}]\}
for some
\omega>0
and with
B^{\mathrm{Br}}
a Brownian bridge is guaranteed, using the separation of
\eta N^{1/3}
at
U_{1}
,
U_{2}
guaranteed on
\mathsf{Fav}
and the control from (
10.3
) on
L^{N}_{k}
in the immediate neighborhoods of
U_{1}
,
U_{2}
included in their definition, by a construction similar to the one used to lower bound
\mathbb E_{\mathcal{F}}[W(\bm{B},L^{N}_{k})]
just above, but with
k=1
; such an
\omega
and the lower bound on the resulting probability depend on only
T,P
, and
\eta
, thus on
\varepsilon
,
T
, and
k
. Combining with (
10.5
), this completes the proof after relabeling
\varepsilon
.
∎

10.2.
Proof of one-point separation

In this section we start the proof of Lemma
10.3
.
In its proof, we will need to know that the
(k-1)
st
curve cannot drop too quickly. This is recorded in the next statement and will be proved in Section
10.3
.

Lemma 10.5

(No quick drops)
.

Fix
k\in\mathbb{N}
.
Suppose for any
T\geq 2
and
\varepsilon>0
there exist
\eta=\eta(\varepsilon,k,T)>0
,
M=M(\varepsilon,k,T)
, and
N_{0}=N_{0}(\varepsilon,k,T)
such that, for any
j\in\llbracket 1,k\rrbracket
(recalling the definition of
\mathsf{Sep}^{N}_{j-2}
from (
9.1
)) and
N\geq N_{0}
,

\mathbb{P}\left(\inf_{xN^{-2/3}\in[-T,T]}\overline{L}^{N}_{j-1}(x)\geq-MN^{1/3}\right)\geq 1-\varepsilon\quad\text{and}\quad\mathbb{P}\left(\mathsf{Sep}^{N}_{j-2}(\eta,[-T,T],\bm{L}^{N})\right)>1-\varepsilon.

Now fix
T\geq 2
,
\eta>0
and
\varepsilon>0
. There exist
\delta=\delta(k,T,\eta,\varepsilon)>0
and
N_{0}=N_{0}(k,T,\varepsilon,\eta)
such that, for any random real number
a\in[-T,T]
that is measurable with respect to
\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{ext}}(k-1,\mathbb{Z},\bm{L}^{N})
(recall Definition
3.14
), i.e., with respect to
(L^{N}_{k},L^{N}_{k+1},\ldots)
, it holds for
N\geq N_{0}
that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\inf_{xN^{-2/3}\in[-\delta,\delta]}\overline{L}^{N}_{k-1}(aN^{2/3}+x)<\overline{L}^{N}_{k-1}(aN^{2/3})-\eta N^{1/3}\right)\leq\varepsilon.

Proof of Lemma
10.3
.

We prove the existence of such a
U_{1}
, as the proof for
U_{2}
follows from reflection into the line
\{x=0\}
and the whole statement then follows from a union bound.

Preliminaries.
Let
\mathsf{RangeCtrl}_{k}(M)=\{\sup_{xN^{-2/3}\in[-2T,2T]}|\overline{L}^{N}_{k}(x)|\leq MN^{1/3}\}
. By hypothesis (
10.1
) and Proposition
10.2
there exists
M=M(T,k,\varepsilon)
such that
\mathbb{P}(\mathsf{RangeCtrl}_{k}(M))\geq 1-\varepsilon
.
For a
P
to be set, let
\mathsf{SlopeCtrl}_{k}
be the event that there exists random
U_{1}\in[-2T,-\frac{3}{2}T]
with

\displaystyle\overline{L}^{N}_{k}(x)

\displaystyle\leq\overline{L}^{N}_{k}(U_{1}N^{2/3})+(x-U_{1}N^{2/3})PN^{-1/3}\quad\text{for }xN^{-2/3}\in[U_{1},U_{1}+1].

We define
U_{1}
by taking it to be the minimum point satisfying the above if there are multiple. By Lemma
10.1
, there exists a deterministic
P=P(M)=P(k,T,\varepsilon)
such that
\mathsf{RangeCtrl}_{k}(M)\subseteq\mathsf{SlopeCtrl}_{k}
and we work on the former event for the remainder of the proof.

Next define (where the below name is short for “single curve separation”), for
k\in\mathbb{N}
, an interval
I
, and a process
X:I\to\mathbb{R}
,

(10.8)

\displaystyle\mathsf{SingSep}^{N}_{k-2}(\eta,I,X):=\left\{\inf_{xN^{-2/3}\in I}\left(L^{N}_{k-2}(x)-X(x)\right)>\eta N^{1/3}\right\}.

We adopt the shorthand
\mathsf{SingSep}^{N}_{k-2}(\eta,L^{N}_{k-1})=\mathsf{SingSep}^{N}_{k-2}(\eta,[-3T,-T],L^{N}_{k-1})
in the rest of this proof.
By hypothesis (
10.2
), we can find
\eta=\eta(T,k,\varepsilon)>0
such that
\smash{\mathbb{P}(\mathsf{SingSep}^{N}_{k-2}(2\eta,L^{N}_{k-1})^{c})}<\smash{\frac{1}{6}}\varepsilon
(though ahead we will actually work on the event
\smash{\mathsf{SingSep}^{N}_{k-2}(\eta,L^{N}_{k-1})}
). Next let

(10.9)

\displaystyle\mathsf{OnePtSep}_{k}^{N}(\delta,L^{N}_{k-1})=\left\{L^{N}_{k-1}(U_{1}N^{2/3})>L^{N}_{k}(U_{1}N^{2/3})+\delta N^{1/3}\right\};

We wish to bound the probability
\mathbb{P}\left(\mathsf{OnePtSep}_{k}^{N}(\delta,L^{N}_{k-1})^{c}\right)
. Let
\mathcal{F}=\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{ext}}(\{k-1\},\llbracket\lfloor-3TN^{2/3}\rfloor
,
\lfloor-TN^{2/3}\rfloor\rrbracket)
be the
\sigma
-algebra generated by
\{L^{N}_{i}(x):(i,x)\not\in\{k-1\}\times\llbracket\lfloor-3TN^{2/3}\rfloor+1,\lfloor-TN^{2/3}\rfloor-1\rrbracket\}
. Notice that
U_{1}
and
L^{N}_{k+1}(U_{1}N^{2/3})
are
\mathcal{F}
-measurable. For
\rho>0
to be specified, define the
\mathcal{F}
-measurable event
\mathsf{Fav}
by

\displaystyle\mathsf{Fav}

\displaystyle:=\left\{\sup_{xN^{-2/3}\in[-3T,-T]}\overline{L}^{N}_{k}(x)\leq MN^{1/3},\min_{xN^{-2/3}\in\{-3T,-T\}}\left(\overline{L}^{N}_{k-2}(x)-\overline{L}^{N}_{k-1}(x)\right)>2\eta N^{1/3}\right\}

\displaystyle\qquad\cap\left\{\min_{xN^{-2/3}\in\{-3T,-T\}}\overline{L}^{N}_{k-1}(x)\geq-MN^{1/3}\right\}

(10.10)

\displaystyle\quad\cap\left\{\min_{zN^{-2/3}\in\{-3T,U_{1}\}}\inf_{xN^{-2/3}\in[0,\rho]}\left(\overline{L}^{N}_{k-2}(z+x)-\overline{L}^{N}_{k-2}(z)\right)>-\tfrac{1}{2}\eta N^{1/3}\right\}

\displaystyle\qquad\cap\left\{\min_{zN^{-2/3}\in\{U_{1},-T\}}\inf_{xN^{-2/3}\in[0,\rho]}\left(\overline{L}^{N}_{k-2}(z-x)-\overline{L}^{N}_{k-2}(z)\right)>-\tfrac{1}{2}\eta N^{1/3}\right\}

\displaystyle\quad\cap\Bigl\{\overline{L}^{N}_{k-2}(U_{1}N^{2/3})>\overline{L}^{N}_{k}(U_{1}N^{2/3})+2\eta N^{1/3}\Bigr\}.

Note that the parameter in the first line of
\mathsf{Fav}
is
2\eta
and not
\eta
as in
\smash{\mathsf{SingSep}^{N}_{k-2}}(\eta,L^{N}_{k-1})
that we work on; this will be needed later to guarantee a little extra space.

Figure 20
.
A depiction of the properties assumed under
\mathsf{Fav}
. The dotted blue curve is
\smash{\overline{L}^{N}_{k-2}}
brought down by
\eta N^{1/3}
, which is what the Bernoulli random walk bridge between the endpoints of
L^{N}_{k-1}
(black dots) that have been conditioned on must avoid under
\mathsf{SingSep}_{k-2}
. This occurs with positive probability because we can find a corridor of width
\eta N^{1/3}
around the starting black points and avoiding
\smash{\overline{L}^{N}_{k}}
and
\smash{\overline{L}^{N}_{k-2}}
because of the other conditions included in
\mathsf{Fav}
.

By Proposition
10.2
(upper bound on
\sup L^{N}_{1}
), the assumed lower tail bound (
10.1
), and the above bound on
\mathbb{P}(\mathsf{SingSep}^{N}_{k-2}(2\eta,L^{N}_{k})^{c})
, we can find
M
such that the probability of the complement of the events in the first two lines is at most
\varepsilon/3
. By Lemma
10.5
we can find
\rho>0
such that the probability of the complement of the events in the third and fourth line is also at most
\varepsilon/3
; finally, the above bound on
\mathbb{P}(\mathsf{SingSep}^{N}_{k-2}(2\eta,L^{N}_{k})^{c})
along with the fact that
\smash{L^{N}_{k-1}\geq L^{N}_{k}}
guarantees that the fifth line’s event’s complement’s probability is at most
\frac{1}{3}\varepsilon
for the same choice of
\eta
as above. Thus overall we have
\mathbb{P}(\mathsf{Fav}^{c})\leq\varepsilon
.

Now we bound
\mathbb{P}(\mathsf{OnePtSep}_{k}^{N}(\delta,L^{N}_{k-1})^{c})
by

(10.11)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\mathsf{OnePtSep}_{k}^{N}(\delta,L^{N}_{k-1})^{c},\mathsf{SingSep}^{N}_{k-2}(\eta,L^{N}_{k-1}),\mathsf{Fav}\right)+2\varepsilon.

Using the Gibbs property and weak monotonicity.
Applying the Gibbs property and letting
B
be a Bernoulli random walk bridge with endpoints
(-3TN^{2/3}
,
L^{N}_{k-1}(-3TN^{2/3}))
and
(-TN^{2/3},L^{N}_{k-1}(-TN^{2/3}))
, the first term in (
10.11
) is equal to (and recalling the definition of
W
from (
3.9
))

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\mathbb E_{\mathcal{F}}\bigl[\mathbbm{1}_{\mathsf{OnePtSep}_{k}^{N}(\delta,B)^{c},\mathsf{SingSep}^{N}_{k-2}(\eta,B)}W(B,L^{N}_{k-2},L^{N}_{k})\bigr]}{\mathbb E_{\mathcal{F}}\left[W(B,L^{N}_{k-2},L^{N}_{k})\right]}\mathbbm{1}_{\mathsf{Fav}}\right]

(10.12)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\mathbb E_{\mathcal{F}}\bigl[\mathbbm{1}_{\mathsf{OnePtSep}_{k}^{N}(\delta,B)^{c}}W(B,L^{N}_{k-2},L^{N}_{k})\mid\mathsf{SingSep}^{N}_{k-2}(\eta,B)\bigr]}{\mathbb E_{\mathcal{F}}\left[W(B,L^{N}_{k-2},L^{N}_{k})\mid\mathsf{SingSep}^{N}_{k-2}(\eta,B)\right]}\mathbbm{1}_{\mathsf{Fav}}\right].

We wish to use weak monotonicity (Corollary
8.5
) to control this expression. This requires us to remove the interaction via
W
with the upper boundary condition
L^{N}_{k-2}
, but we may include a non-intersection interaction with an upper curve. The following argument first aims to use the upper non-intersection freedom to remove the upper
W
-interaction.

Write
W(B,L^{N}_{k-2},L^{N}_{k})=W_{\mathrm{up}}(B,L^{N}_{k-2})\cdot W_{\mathrm{low}}(B,L^{N}_{k})
(as in Definition
3.15
). Then we see by Lemma
9.1
that, when
B
satisfies
\mathsf{SingSep}^{N}_{k-2}(\eta,B)
,

\displaystyle W_{\mathrm{up}}(B,L^{N}_{k-2})\geq(1-q^{\eta N^{1/3}})^{2TN^{2/3}}\geq\tfrac{1}{2}

almost surely for all large enough
N
.
Thus

\displaystyle\mathbb E_{\mathcal{F}}\left[W(B,L^{N}_{k-2},L^{N}_{k})\mid\mathsf{SingSep}^{N}_{k-2}(\eta,B)\right]\geq\tfrac{1}{2}\mathbb{E}\left[W_{\mathrm{low}}(B,L^{N}_{k})\mid\mathsf{SingSep}^{N}_{k-2}(\eta,B)\right].

Let
B\geq L^{N}_{k}
be shorthand for the event
B(x)\geq L^{N}_{k}(x)
for all
xN^{-2/3}\in[-3T,-T]
. By using the previous display and that
W_{\mathrm{up}}(B,L^{N}_{k-2})\leq 1
, we see that (
10.12
) is upper bounded by

\displaystyle 2\cdot\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\mathbb E_{\mathcal{F}}\bigl[\mathbbm{1}_{\mathsf{OnePtSep}_{k}^{N}(\delta,B)^{c}}W_{\mathrm{low}}(B,L^{N}_{k})\mid\mathsf{SingSep}^{N}_{k-2}(\eta,B)\bigr]}{\mathbb E_{\mathcal{F}}\left[W_{\mathrm{low}}(B,L^{N}_{k})\mid\mathsf{SingSep}^{N}_{k-2}(\eta,B)\right]}\mathbbm{1}_{\mathsf{Fav}}\right]

(10.13)

\displaystyle=2\cdot\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\mathbb E_{\mathcal{F}}\bigl[\mathbbm{1}_{\mathsf{OnePtSep}_{k}^{N}(\delta,B)^{c}}W_{\mathrm{low}}(B,L^{N}_{k})\mid B\geq L^{N}_{k},\mathsf{SingSep}^{N}_{k-2}(\eta,B)\bigr]}{\mathbb E_{\mathcal{F}}\left[W_{\mathrm{low}}(B,L^{N}_{k})\mid B\geq L^{N}_{k},\mathsf{SingSep}^{N}_{k-2}(\eta,B)\right]}\mathbbm{1}_{\mathsf{Fav}}\right],

the equality due to the fact that
W_{\mathrm{low}}(B,L^{N}_{k})=0
if
B(x)<L^{N}_{k}(x)
for any
xN^{-2/3}\in[-3T,-T]
and since
B\geq L^{N}_{k}
has positive (though
a priori
possibly tending to
0
with
N\to\infty
) probability.
We put
B\geq L^{N}_{k}
in the conditioning because we need it to ensure that
B(U_{1}N^{2/3})
does not come too close to
L^{N}_{k}(U_{1}N^{2/3})
(the other interaction of
B
with
L^{N}_{k}
via
W
will next be removed by weak monotonicity).

The display (
10.13
) now equals

\displaystyle 2\cdot\mathbb{E}\biggl[\frac{\mathbb E_{\mathcal{F}}\bigl[W_{\mathrm{low}}(B,L^{N}_{k})\mid B\geq L^{N}_{k},\mathsf{SingSep}^{N}_{k-2}(\eta,B),\mathsf{OnePtSep}_{k}^{N}(\delta,B)^{c}\bigr]}{\mathbb E_{\mathcal{F}}\left[W_{\mathrm{low}}(B,L^{N}_{k})\mid B\geq L^{N}_{k},\mathsf{SingSep}^{N}_{k-2}(\eta,B)\right]}

\displaystyle\times\mathbb P_{\mathcal{F}}\Bigl(\mathsf{OnePtSep}_{k}^{N}(\delta,B)^{c}\ \Big|\ B\geq L^{N}_{k},\mathsf{SingSep}^{N}_{k-2}(\eta,B)\Bigr)\mathbbm{1}_{\mathsf{Fav}}\biggr].

Note that
\mathsf{SingSep}^{N}_{k-2}(\eta,B)
is simply an upper non-intersection boundary condition for
B
and
\mathsf{OnePtSep}_{k}^{N}(\delta,B)^{c}
is a one-point lower tail event at a deterministic location when conditioned on
\mathcal{F}
. Thus by the weak monotonicity statement Corollary
8.5
, the first ratio in the previous display is upper bounded by
c(q)^{-1}
. Also invoking monotonicity of non-intersecting Bernoulli random walk bridges (Lemma
8.2
), the previous display is upper bounded by

(10.14)

\displaystyle 2c(q)^{-1}\cdot\mathbb{E}\biggl[\mathbb P_{\mathcal{F}}\Bigl(\mathsf{OnePtSep}_{k}^{N}(\delta,B)^{c}\ \Big|\ B(U_{1}N^{2/3})\geq L^{N}_{k}(U_{1}N^{2/3}),\mathsf{SingSep}^{N}_{k-2}(\eta,B)\Bigr)\mathbbm{1}_{\mathsf{Fav}}\biggr].

Figure 21
.
A depiction of the argument lower bounding (
10.15
). The green curve is the Bernoulli random walk bridge
B
, and the dashed red lines are curves which it must avoid. In each of the intervals of size
\rho N^{2/3}
(one to the right of
-3TN^{2/3}
, one on either side of
U_{1}N^{2/3}
, and one to the left of
-TN^{2/3}
),
\smash{\overline{L}^{N}_{k-2}}
falls from the relevant boundary point by at most
\frac{1}{2}\eta N^{1/3}
. The bottom black dot at location
U_{1}N^{2/3}
is the value of
\smash{\overline{L}^{N}_{k}(U_{1}N^{2/3})}
.

Simplifying the conditioning.
We claim, on
\mathsf{Fav}
, that
\mathbb P_{\mathcal{F}}(\mathsf{SingSep}^{N}_{k-2}(\eta,B)\mid B(U_{1}N^{2/3})\geq L^{N}_{k}(U_{1}N^{2/3}))\geq\alpha
for some
\alpha=\alpha(\varepsilon,k,T)>0
. First, it is sufficient to lower bound

(10.15)

\mathbb P_{\mathcal{F}}\left(\mathsf{SingSep}^{N}_{k-2}(\eta,B),B(U_{1}N^{2/3})\geq L^{N}_{k}(U_{1}N^{2/3})\right).

See Figure
21
. Recall the definition (
10.10
) of
\mathsf{Fav}
, and that
B
is a Bernoulli random walk bridge between
(-3TN^{2/3}
,
L^{N}_{k-1}(-3TN^{2/3}))
and
(-TN^{2/3},L^{N}_{k-1}(-TN^{2/3}))
. On
\mathsf{Fav}
, the separation of
B
from
L^{N}_{k-2}
is at least
2\eta N^{1/3}
at
-3TN^{2/3}
and
-TN^{2/3}
;
\smash{\overline{L}^{N}_{k-2}}
falls from its values at
-3TN^{2/3}
,
-TN^{2/3}
, and
U_{1}N^{2/3}
by at most
\frac{1}{2}\eta N^{1/3}
in an interval of size
\rho N^{2/3}
on either side of those values (if that interval lies inside
[-3T,-T]
); the separation of
L^{N}_{k-2}
from
L^{N}_{k}
at
U_{1}N^{2/3}
is at least
2\eta N^{1/3}
; and
\smash{\overline{L}^{N}_{k-2}}\geq-MN^{1/3}
.

Let
\overline{B}(x)=B(x)-px
.
By the above, the event
\{\mathsf{SingSep}^{N}_{k-2}(\eta,B),B(U_{1}N^{2/3})\geq L^{N}_{k}(U_{1}N^{2/3})\}
occurs if, (i)
\overline{B}
falls from
\overline{B}(-3TN^{2/3})
at
-3TN^{2/3}
to
-(M+4\eta)N^{1/3}
at
(-3T+\rho)N^{2/3}
while not going above
\overline{L}^{N}_{k-2}(-3TN^{2/3})-\frac{3}{4}\eta N^{1/3}
in
[-3TN^{2/3},(-3T+\rho)N^{2/3}]
; (ii) stays below
-(M+3\eta)N^{1/3}
on
[(-3T+\rho)N^{2/3},(U_{1}-\rho)N^{2/3}]
; (iii) jumps up to lie in
[\overline{L}^{N}_{k}(U_{1}N^{2/3}),\overline{L}^{N}_{k}(U_{1}N^{2/3})+\eta N^{1/3}]
at
U_{1}N^{2/3}
while not exceeding
\overline{L}^{N}_{k-2}(U_{1}N^{2/3})-\frac{3}{4}\eta N^{1/3}
on
[(U_{1}-\rho)N^{2/3},U_{1}N^{2/3}]
; and repeating similar properties in reverse on
[U_{1}N^{2/3},-TN^{2/3}]
. Since on
\mathsf{Fav}
it holds that
\smash{\overline{L}^{N}_{k}}(U_{1}N^{2/3})\leq MN^{1/3}
and
\inf_{xN^{-2/3}\in\{-3T,-T\}N^{2/3}}\overline{L}^{N}_{k-1}(x)\geq-MN^{1/3}
, it follows that the probability of this event is indeed lower bounded by a positive constant
\alpha=\alpha(M,\eta,T,\rho)=\alpha(\varepsilon,k,T)
.

Thus (
10.14
) is upper bounded by

\displaystyle 2c(q)^{-1}\cdot\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\mathbb P_{\mathcal{F}}\left(\mathsf{OnePtSep}_{k}^{N}(\delta,B)^{c}\mid B(U_{1}N^{2/3})\geq L^{N}_{k}(U_{1}N^{2/3})\right)}{\mathbb P_{\mathcal{F}}(\mathsf{SingSep}^{N}_{k-2}(\eta,B)\mid B(U_{1}N^{2/3})\geq L^{N}_{k}(U_{1}N^{2/3}))}\mathbbm{1}_{\mathsf{Fav}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq 2c(q)^{-1}\alpha^{-1}\cdot\mathbb{E}\Bigl[\mathbb P_{\mathcal{F}}\left(\mathsf{OnePtSep}_{k}^{N}(\delta,B)^{c}\mid B(U_{1}N^{2/3})\geq L^{N}_{k}(U_{1}N^{2/3})\right)\mathbbm{1}_{\mathsf{Fav}}\Bigr].

Invoking a Gaussian comparison.
It is not hard to see that for small enough
\delta
, the previous display is smaller than
\varepsilon
. Indeed, recalling the definition (
10.9
) of
\mathsf{OnePtSep}^{N}_{k}
and letting
X\sim\mathcal{N}(0,1)
, by a normal approximation Bernoulli random walk bridges (Lemma
6.14
), for large enough
N
(depending on
\delta
), the previous display is upper bounded by

(10.16)

\displaystyle 2c(q)^{-1}\alpha^{-1}\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbb P_{\mathcal{F}}\left(\sigma X+\mu\leq\delta N^{1/3}\ \Big|\ \sigma X+\mu>0\right)\mathbbm{1}_{\mathsf{Fav}}\right]+\tfrac{1}{2}\varepsilon,

where

\displaystyle\mu

\displaystyle:=\frac{U_{1}+3T}{2T}\overline{L}^{N}_{k-1}(-TN^{2/3})-\frac{T+U_{1}}{2T}\overline{L}^{N}_{k-1}(-3TN^{2/3})-\overline{L}^{N}_{k}(U_{1}N^{2/3})\quad\text{and}

\displaystyle\sigma^{2}

\displaystyle:=p(1-p)\cdot\frac{(U_{1}+3T)(-T-U_{1})N^{2/3}}{2T};

the factor of
p(1-p)
in
\sigma^{2}
arises, as explained after Lemma
6.14
, since
B
is a Bernoulli random walk bridge with slope
p
, making its increments have variance approximately
p(1-p)
. Since
\overline{L}^{N}_{k-1}(-3TN^{2/3})
,
\overline{L}^{N}_{k-1}(-TN^{2/3})>-MN^{1/3}
and
\overline{L}^{N}_{k}(U_{1}N^{2/3})\leq MN^{1/3}
on
\mathsf{Fav}
, it follows that, again on
\mathsf{Fav}
,

\mu\geq-2MN^{1/3}.

Since
U_{1}\in[-2T,-\tfrac{3}{2}T]
, it follows that

\sigma^{2}\geq\tfrac{3}{8}p(1-p)TN^{2/3}.

Let
K=\tfrac{3}{8}p(1-p)T
. Recall from Lemma
8.1
that
\mathbb P_{\mathcal{F}}\left(X<r+\delta N^{1/3}\mid X>r\right)
is increasing as a function of
r
for any fixed
\delta>0
and
N
. Since
-\sigma^{-1}\mu\leq(\frac{8}{3}(p(1-p)T)^{-1})^{1/2}2M=2K^{-1/2}M
, (
10.16
) is upper bounded by

\displaystyle 2c(q)^{-1}\alpha^{-1}\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbb P_{\mathcal{F}}\left(X\leq-\sigma^{-1}\mu+\sigma^{-1}\delta N^{1/3}\mid X>-\sigma^{-1}\mu\right)\mathbbm{1}_{\mathsf{Fav}}\right]+\tfrac{1}{2}\varepsilon,

\displaystyle\leq 2c(q)^{-1}\alpha^{-1}\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbb P_{\mathcal{F}}\left(X\leq-\sigma^{-1}\mu+K^{-1/2}\delta\ \Big|\ X>-\sigma^{-1}\mu\right)\mathbbm{1}_{\mathsf{Fav}}\right]+\tfrac{1}{2}\varepsilon

\displaystyle\leq 2c(q)^{-1}\alpha^{-1}\mathbb{P}\left(X\leq 2K^{-1/2}M+K^{-1/2}\delta\ \Big|\ X>2K^{-1/2}M\right)+\tfrac{1}{2}\varepsilon.

It is now a simple computation using standard normal bounds that the first term is upper bounded by
\tfrac{1}{2}\varepsilon
if
\delta
is made small enough (independent of
N
). Returning to (
10.11
), we see that we have bounded
\mathbb{P}(\mathsf{OnePtSep}^{N}_{k}(\delta,L^{N}_{k-1})^{c})
by
3\varepsilon
. This completes the proof after relabeling
\varepsilon
.
∎

10.3.
No quick drops

In this section we prove Lemma
10.5
.
We first establish a one-point version of this statement, which will then be upgraded via a chaining argument. To state this one-point version we introduce notation for two events.
First, we use the shorthand
\mathsf{SingSep}^{N}_{k-2}(\eta,L^{N}_{k-1}):=\mathsf{SingSep}^{N}_{k-2}(\eta,[-2T,2T],L^{N}_{k-1})
with the latter as defined in (
10.8
).
Second, for any real numbers
M,\eta_{0},\delta_{0}>0
, let
\mathsf{Fav}=\mathsf{Fav}(M,\eta_{0},\delta_{0})
be defined by

(10.17)

\begin{split}\mathsf{Fav}&:=\Bigl\{\min_{xN^{-2/3}\in\{a,2T\}}\overline{L}^{N}_{k-1}(x)>-MN^{1/3},\inf_{xN^{-2/3}\in[-2T,2T]}\overline{L}^{N}_{k-2}(x)\geq-MN^{1/3}\Bigr\}\\
&\qquad\cap\left\{\min_{xN^{-2/3}\in\{a,2T\}}\left(\overline{L}^{N}_{k-2}(x)-\overline{L}^{N}_{k-1}(x)\right)\geq 4\eta_{0}N^{1/3}\right\}\\
&\qquad\cap\left\{\inf_{xN^{-2/3}\in[0,\delta_{0}]}\overline{L}^{N}_{k-2}(aN^{2/3}+x)>\overline{L}^{N}_{k-2}(aN^{2/3})-\tfrac{1}{2}\eta_{0}N^{1/3}\right\}\\
&\qquad\cap\left\{\inf_{xN^{-2/3}\in[0,\delta_{0}]}\overline{L}^{N}_{k-2}(2TN^{2/3}-x)>\overline{L}^{N}_{k-2}(2TN^{2/3})-\tfrac{1}{2}\eta_{0}N^{1/3}\right\}.\end{split}

Lemma 10.6
.

Fix
M
,
T\geq 2
,
\delta_{0}>0
and
0<\eta<\frac{1}{2}\eta_{0}
. There exist
c=c(M,T)>0
and
C=C(M,T,\eta_{0},\delta_{0})
such that, for any random real number
a\in[-T,T]
that is measurable with respect to
(L^{N}_{k},L^{N}_{k+1},\ldots)
and any
\delta>0
and
N\geq 1
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\Bigl(\overline{L}^{N}_{k-1}((a+\delta)N^{2/3})<\overline{L}^{N}_{k-1}(aN^{2/3})-\eta N^{1/3},\mathsf{Fav}(M,\eta_{0},\delta_{0}),\mathsf{SingSep}^{N}_{k-2}(2\eta_{0},L^{N}_{k-1})\Bigr)

\displaystyle\leq C\exp\left(-c\frac{\eta^{2}}{|\delta|}\right).

Proof of Lemma
10.5
.

Recall that we must bound
\mathbb{P}(\inf_{xN^{-2/3}\in[-\delta,\delta]}L^{N}_{k-1}(aN^{2/3}+x)<L^{N}_{k-1}(x))
. We will replace the interval
[-\delta,\delta]
by
[0,\delta]
in the following argument; the case of
[-\delta,0]
follows by reflection in the line
\{x=0\}
, and the case of
[-\delta,\delta]
then follows by a union bound.

Fix
\eta>0
. For any
j\in\mathbb{N}
,
\delta>0
, and random real number
a
which is measurable with respect to
(L^{N}_{j+1},L^{N}_{j+2},\ldots)
, let the event
\mathsf{Drop}^{N}_{j}(a,\delta,\eta)
be defined by

\displaystyle\mathsf{Drop}^{N}_{j}(a,\delta,\eta)=\left\{\inf_{xN^{-2/3}\in[0,\delta]}\overline{L}^{N}_{j}(aN^{2/3}+x)<\overline{L}^{N}_{j}(aN^{2/3})-\eta N^{1/3}\right\}.

We prove the lemma by induction on
j\in\llbracket 1,k-1\rrbracket
. So we may assume that for any
T\geq 2
,
\varepsilon>0
, and
\eta>0
there exists
\delta_{0}
such that, for any
a\in[-2T,2T]
that is measurable with respect to
(L^{N}_{j},L^{N}_{j+1},\ldots)
,

(10.18)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\mathsf{Drop}^{N}_{j-1}(a,\delta_{0},\eta)\right)\leq\varepsilon.

Then we must show that, for any fixed
T\geq 2
,
\eta>0
and
\varepsilon>0
, there exists
\delta>0
such that, for any
a\in[-T,T]
that is measurable with respect to
(L^{N}_{j+1},L^{N}_{j+2},\ldots)
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\mathsf{Drop}^{N}_{j}(a,\delta,\eta)\right)\leq\varepsilon.

Note that it is sufficient to prove this for a smaller
\eta
if needed since
\mathbb{P}(\mathsf{Drop}^{N}_{j}(a,\delta,\eta))
is decreasing in
\eta
.

We prove the base case
j=1
and the induction step simultaneously (in the case of
j=1
the induction hypothesis (
10.18
) holds trivially).
Our argument is by a chaining argument to upgrade the one-point bound from Lemma
10.6
to a uniform bound. Let
x_{i,\ell}=a+i2^{-\ell}\delta
for
i=0,1,\ldots,2^{\ell}
and define

\displaystyle\mathsf{E}_{\ell}:=\bigcap_{i=1}^{2^{\ell}}\Bigl\{\overline{L}^{N}_{j}(x_{i,\ell}N^{2/3})>\overline{L}^{N}_{j}(x_{\lfloor i/2\rfloor,\ell-1}N^{2/3})-\alpha_{\ell}\eta N^{1/3}\Bigr\},

where
\alpha_{\ell}:=\rho 2^{-\ell/4}

with
\rho:=\frac{1}{4}(\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}2^{-j/4})^{-1}
(which is clearly positive), so that
\sum_{\ell=0}^{\infty}\alpha_{\ell}<\frac{1}{4}
.
We claim that, with
\ell_{\max}=\lceil\frac{1}{2}\log_{2}N\rceil
,

(10.19)

\displaystyle\mathsf{Drop}^{N}_{j}(a,\delta,\eta)\subseteq\bigcup_{\ell=0}^{\ell_{\max}}(\mathsf{E}_{\ell})^{c}.

Indeed, it holds that
(x_{i+1,\ell_{\max}}-x_{i,\ell_{\max}})N^{2/3}=\delta N^{2/3-1/2}=\delta N^{1/6}
; since
L^{N}_{j}
is a Bernoulli path, this implies that, on the complement of the righthand side of the previous display, i.e., on
\cap_{\ell=0}^{\ell_{\max}}\mathsf{E}_{\ell}
, and for all large enough
N
(depending on
\eta
,
\delta
),

\displaystyle\inf_{xN^{-2/3}\in[0,\delta]}\overline{L}^{N}_{j}(aN^{2/3}+x)

\displaystyle\geq\inf_{\begin{subarray}{c}i=1,\ldots,2^{\ell}\\
\ell=0,\ldots,\ell_{\max}\end{subarray}}\overline{L}^{N}_{j}(x_{i,\ell}N^{2/3})-\delta N^{1/6}

\displaystyle>\inf_{\begin{subarray}{c}i=1,\ldots,2^{\ell}\\
\ell=0,\ldots,\ell_{\max}\end{subarray}}\overline{L}^{N}_{j}(x_{i,\ell}N^{2/3})-\tfrac{1}{2}\eta N^{1/3}.

For any
i
and
\ell
, setting
i_{m+1}:=\lfloor i_{m}/2\rfloor
for
m\geq 1
and
i_{0}=i
, again on
\cap_{\ell=0}^{\ell_{\max}}\mathsf{E}_{\ell}
,

\displaystyle\overline{L}^{N}_{j}(x_{i,\ell}N^{2/3})

\displaystyle=\sum_{m=0}^{\ell}\overline{L}^{N}_{j}(x_{i_{m},\ell-m}N^{2/3})-\overline{L}^{N}_{j}(x_{i_{m+1},\ell-m-1}N^{2/3})+\overline{L}^{N}_{j}(aN^{2/3})

\displaystyle\geq\overline{L}^{N}_{j}(aN^{2/3})-\sum_{m=0}^{\ell}\alpha_{\ell-m}\eta N^{1/3}\geq\overline{L}^{N}_{j}(aN^{2/3})-\tfrac{1}{4}\eta N^{1/3}

by the definition of
\alpha_{\ell}
. So, on
\cap_{\ell=0}^{\ell_{\max}}E_{\ell}
, it indeed holds that
\inf_{xN^{-2/3}\in[0,\delta]}\smash{\overline{L}^{N}_{j}(aN^{2/3}+x)}>\smash{\overline{L}^{N}_{j}(aN^{2/3})}-\eta N^{1/3}
, i.e., (
10.19
) holds.

Let
\mathsf{SingSep}^{N}_{j-1}(2\eta_{0})=\mathsf{SingSep}^{N}_{j-1}(2\eta_{0},[-2T,2T],L^{N}_{j})
be as defined in (
10.8
). Let
\eta_{0}>0
be such that
\mathbb{P}(\mathsf{SingSep}^{N}_{j-1}(2\eta_{0}))
, as well as the probability of the second event in (
10.17
), is at least
\smash{1-\frac{1}{8}\varepsilon}
, which is possible by the assumptions in Lemma
10.5
. By (
10.18
), let
\delta_{0}>0
be such that

\mathbb{P}\left(\mathsf{Drop}^{N}_{j-1}(a,\delta_{0},\tfrac{1}{2}\eta_{0})\cup\mathsf{Drop}^{N}_{j-1}(2T,\delta_{0},\tfrac{1}{2}\eta_{0})\right)\leq\tfrac{1}{8}\varepsilon.

Let
\mathsf{Fav}(M,\eta_{0},\delta_{0})
be as in Lemma
10.6
, i.e., (
10.17
). Now, by the assumptions in Lemma
10.5
and these choices, performing a union bound shows that we may choose
M
such that
\mathbb{P}(\mathsf{Fav}(M,\eta_{0},\delta_{0})^{c}\cup\mathsf{SingSep}^{N}_{j-1}(2\eta_{0})^{c})\leq\frac{1}{2}\varepsilon
. Reduce
\eta
if necessary so that it is less than
\frac{1}{2}\eta_{0}
. Then, by (
10.19
),

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\mathsf{Drop}^{N}_{j}(a,\delta,\eta)\right)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\left(\mathsf{Drop}^{N}_{j}(a,\delta,\eta),\mathsf{Fav}(M,\eta_{0},\delta_{0}),\mathsf{SingSep}^{N}_{j-1}(2\eta_{0})\right)+\tfrac{1}{2}\varepsilon

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{\ell=0}^{\ell_{\max}}\mathbb{P}\left(\mathsf{E}_{\ell}^{c},\mathsf{Fav}(M,\eta_{0},\delta_{0}),\mathsf{SingSep}^{N}_{j-1}(2\eta_{0})\right)+\tfrac{1}{2}\varepsilon.

By Lemma
10.6
and a union bound over the
2^{\ell}
events in
\mathsf{E}_{\ell}^{c}
, we know that the sum in the previous display is upper bounded, for
\delta>0
, by

\displaystyle\sum_{\ell=0}^{\infty}2^{\ell}C\exp\left(-c\alpha_{\ell}^{2}\eta^{2}/(2^{-\ell}\delta)\right)=\sum_{\ell=0}^{\infty}2^{\ell}C\exp\left(-c\delta^{-1}\rho^{2}2^{-\ell/2+\ell}\eta^{2}\right).

This is upper bounded by
C\exp(-c\delta^{-1}\eta^{2})
.
Setting
\delta>0
small enough such that this is upper bounded by
\frac{1}{2}\varepsilon
completes the proof.
∎

Next we turn to establishing the one-point statement of there being no quick drops (Lemma
10.6
).

Proof of Lemma
10.6
.

Note that
a
is measurable with respect to
(\smash{L^{N}_{k},L^{N}_{k+1}},\ldots)
by hypothesis, so
[aN^{2/3},2TN^{2/3}]
forms a stopping domain (Definition
6.1
) with respect to
(\smash{L^{N}_{1},\ldots,L^{N}_{k-1}})
. Let
\mathcal{F}=\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{ext}}(\{k-1\},\llbracket aN^{2/3},2TN^{2/3}\rrbracket,\bm{L}^{N})
be as in that definition, i.e., it is the
\sigma
-algebra generated by
\{L^{N}_{i}(x):(i,x)\not\in\{k-1\}\times\llbracket aN^{2/3}+1,TN^{2/3}-1\rrbracket\}
.
Note also that
\mathsf{Fav}
(
10.17
) is
\mathcal{F}
-measurable, and recall
\mathsf{SingSep}^{N}_{k-2}
from (
10.8
). Let
B
be a Bernoulli random walk bridge from
(aN^{2/3},L^{N}_{k-1}(aN^{2/3}))
to
(2TN^{2/3},L^{N}_{k-1}(2TN^{2/3}))
and
\overline{B}(x)=B(x)-px
. By the strong Hall-Littlewood Gibbs property (Proposition
6.2
), the probability in the statement of the lemma equals

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\mathbb E_{\mathcal{F}}\Bigl[\mathbbm{1}_{\overline{B}((a+\delta)N^{2/3})<\overline{L}^{N}_{k-1}(aN^{2/3})-\eta N^{1/3},\mathsf{SingSep}^{N}_{k-2}(2\eta_{0},B)}W(B,L^{N}_{k-2},L^{N}_{k})\Bigr]}{\mathbb E_{\mathcal{F}}[W(B,L^{N}_{k-2},L^{N}_{k})]}\mathbbm{1}_{\mathsf{Fav}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\mathbb E_{\mathcal{F}}[\mathbbm{1}_{\overline{B}((a+\delta)N^{2/3})<\overline{L}^{N}_{k-1}(aN^{2/3})-\eta N^{1/3},}W(B,L^{N}_{k-2},L^{N}_{k})\mid\mathsf{SingSep}^{N}_{k-2}(2\eta_{0},B)]}{\mathbb E_{\mathcal{F}}[W(B,L^{N}_{k-2},L^{N}_{k})\mid\mathsf{SingSep}^{N}_{k-2}(2\eta_{0},B)]}\mathbbm{1}_{\mathsf{Fav}}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\biggl[\frac{\mathbb E_{\mathcal{F}}[W(B,L^{N}_{k-2},L^{N}_{k})\mid\mathsf{SingSep}^{N}_{k-2}(2\eta_{0},B),\overline{B}((a+\delta)N^{2/3})<\overline{L}^{N}_{k-1}(aN^{2/3})-\eta N^{1/3}]}{\mathbb E_{\mathcal{F}}[W(B,L^{N}_{k-2},L^{N}_{k})\mid\mathsf{SingSep}^{N}_{k-2}(2\eta_{0},B)]}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\times\mathbb P_{\mathcal{F}}\left(\overline{B}((a+\delta)N^{2/3})<\overline{L}^{N}_{k-1}(aN^{2/3})-\eta N^{1/3}\mid\mathsf{SingSep}^{N}_{k-2}(2\eta_{0},B)\right)\mathbbm{1}_{\mathsf{Fav}}\biggr].

Now, on
\mathsf{SingSep}^{N}_{k-2}(2\eta_{0},B)
, it holds by Lemma
9.1
that
W_{\mathrm{up}}(B,L^{N}_{k-2})\geq\frac{1}{2}
(recall from Definition
3.15
) for all large enough
N
, and
W_{\mathrm{up}}(B,L^{N}_{k-2})\leq 1
trivially. Thus the previous display is upper bounded by

\displaystyle 2\cdot\mathbb{E}\biggl[\frac{\mathbb E_{\mathcal{F}}[W(B,L^{N}_{k})\mid\mathsf{SingSep}^{N}_{k-2}(2\eta_{0},B),\overline{B}((a+\delta)N^{2/3})<\overline{L}^{N}_{k-1}(aN^{2/3})-\eta N^{1/3}]}{\mathbb E_{\mathcal{F}}[W(B,L^{N}_{k})\mid\mathsf{SingSep}^{N}_{k-2}(2\eta_{0},B)]}

\displaystyle\times\mathbb P_{\mathcal{F}}\left(\overline{B}((a+\delta)N^{2/3})<\overline{L}^{N}_{k-1}(aN^{2/3})-\eta N^{1/3}\mid\mathsf{SingSep}^{N}_{k-2}(2\eta_{0},B)\right)\mathbbm{1}_{\mathsf{Fav}}\biggr].

By Corollary
8.5
, the first factor in the previous line is upper bounded by
c(q)^{-1}
, yielding that the previous display is upper bounded by

\displaystyle 2c(q)^{-1}\mathbb{E}\biggl[\mathbb P_{\mathcal{F}}\Bigl(\overline{B}\bigl((a+\delta)N^{2/3}\bigr)<\overline{L}^{N}_{k-1}(aN^{2/3})-\eta N^{1/3}\ \Big|\ \mathsf{SingSep}^{N}_{k-2}(2\eta_{0},B)\Bigr)\mathbbm{1}_{\mathsf{Fav}}\biggr].

We wish to remove the conditioning event, for which we first lower bound its probability, on
\mathsf{Fav}
. Now, on
\mathsf{Fav}
, the separation between
\smash{\overline{B}}
and
\smash{\overline{L}^{N}_{k-2}}
at
aN^{2/3}
and
2TN^{2/3}
is at least
4\eta_{0}N^{1/3}
. Further,
\smash{\overline{L}^{N}_{k-2}}
drops by at most
\smash{\frac{1}{2}\eta_{0}N^{1/3}}
in
[aN^{2/3},(a+\delta_{0})N^{2/3}]
and
[(2T-\delta_{0})N^{2/3},2TN^{2/3}]
and remains above
-MN^{1/3}
on all of
[-2TN^{2/3},2TN^{2/3}]
. Thus for
\overline{B}
to maintain a separation of
2\eta_{0}N^{1/3}
with
\smash{\overline{L}^{N}_{k-2}}
on
[-2TN^{2/3},2TN^{2/3}]
, it is sufficient if it does the following (similar to Figure
21
in the proof of Lemma
10.3
): drops to or below
-(M+4\eta_{0})N^{1/3}
in
[aN^{2/3},(a+\delta_{0})N^{2/3}]
and never goes above
\smash{\overline{L}^{N}_{k-1}}(aN^{2/3})+\frac{1}{2}\eta_{0}N^{1/3}
in the same interval, stays below
-(M+3\eta_{0})N^{1/3}
on
[(a+\delta_{0})N^{2/3},(2T-\delta_{0})N^{2/3}]
, and jumps up to
\smash{\overline{L}^{N}_{k-1}}(2TN^{2/3})
at
2TN^{2/3}
while staying below
\overline{L}^{N}_{k-1}(2TN^{2/3})+\frac{1}{2}\eta_{0}N^{1/3}
on
[(2T-\delta_{0})N^{2/3},2TN^{2/3}]
. The probability that
\overline{B}
satisfies these conditions is lower bounded by some deterministic
\alpha=\alpha(T,\delta_{0},M,\eta_{0})>0
(since the same is true for the Brownian bridge weak limit of the rescaled
\overline{B}
), using also that
\smash{\overline{L}^{N}_{k-1}}(aN^{2/3}),\smash{\overline{L}^{N}_{k-1}}(2TN^{2/3})\in[-MN^{1/3},MN^{1/3}]
on
\mathsf{Fav}
. This yields that the previous display is upper bounded by

\displaystyle 2\alpha^{-1}c(q)^{-1}\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbb P_{\mathcal{F}}\Bigl(\overline{B}((a+\delta)N^{2/3})<\overline{L}^{N}_{k-1}(aN^{2/3})-\eta N^{1/3}\Bigr)\mathbbm{1}_{\mathsf{Fav}}\right].

Since
\overline{B}(2TN^{2/3}),\overline{B}(aN^{2/3})\in[-MN^{1/3},MN^{1/3}]
(so that the endpoints of
B
define a line of slope
p+O(1)MN^{-1/3}
), it follows by one-point bounds on Bernoulli random walk bridges (Lemma
6.13
) that, for all
\delta>0
, the previous display is upper bounded by

\displaystyle C\alpha^{-1}c(q)^{-1}\exp\left(-c\delta^{-1}\eta^{2}\right).

This completes the proof.
∎

11.
Partition function and non-intersection probability estimates

In this section we prove Proposition
6.12
. The argument is fairly standard given what we have already done; it first appeared in
[
CH14
]
, and has been used in a number of subsequent works
[
CH16
,
CD18
,
DFF+21
,
DFF+21
,
BCD23
]
.
It relies on sampling from a measure on paths on a larger interval where the interaction between paths is turned off in the interval of interest and considering the Radon-Nikodym derivative of this measure with the original one; if one has a probability lower bound on the partition function of the new measure, it can be made into a high probability lower bound on the partition function of the original measure, by an argument that can be understood as a form of “size-biased” sampling.

However, our arguments are slightly more complicated than in earlier works as the larger interval we work on is
[U_{1},U_{2}]
(defined as in Lemma
10.3
such that there is control on the growth of the lower curve near
U_{i}
) and so is random. This is because we need some such control on the lower curve at the boundaries of the interval to obtain a lower bound on the partition function for the new measure mentioned above. In the earlier works, this lower bound was achieved on a deterministic interval, using the stochastic monotonicity and other features of the Gibbs property that were available in those models.

As in the last two sections, we assume here that
L^{N}_{k}
satisfies Assumptions
1
and
2
without explicitly mentioning it in the statements.

11.1.
Lower bounding the partition function

Fix
T\geq 2
. Let
U_{1}\in[-2T,-\frac{3}{2}T]
be the largest point and
U_{2}\in[\frac{3}{2}T,2T]
be the smallest point such that

(11.1)

\begin{split}\overline{L}^{N}_{k+1}(x)&\leq\overline{L}^{N}_{k+1}(U_{1}N^{2/3})+(x-U_{1}N^{2/3})PN^{-1/3}\quad\text{for}\quad xN^{-2/3}\in[U_{1},U_{1}+1]\\
\overline{L}^{N}_{k+1}(x)&\leq\overline{L}^{N}_{k+1}(U_{2}N^{2/3})+(U_{2}N^{2/3}-x)PN^{-1/3}\quad\text{for}\quad xN^{-2/3}\in[U_{2}-1,U_{2}],\end{split}

for a
P
to be specified later. In the event that such a
U_{1}
does not exist, we set it to
-2T
, and in the event that such a
U_{2}
does not exist, we set it to
2T
.

Fix an interval
[-\frac{3}{2}T,\frac{3}{2}T]\subseteq[a,b]\subseteq[-2T,2T]
and
\bm{x},\bm{y}\in\{\bm{z}\in\mathbb{R}^{k}:z_{1}>\ldots>z_{k}\}
. We define a discrete line ensemble (Definition
3.6
)
\smash{\tilde{\bm{L}}^{N}=(\tilde{L}^{N}_{1},\ldots,\tilde{L}^{N}_{k})}
, where each
\tilde{L}^{N}_{i}:\llbracket aN^{2/3},bN^{2/3}\rrbracket\to\mathbb{R}
with
\tilde{L}^{N}_{i}(aN^{2/3})=x_{i}
and
\tilde{L}^{N}_{i}(bN^{2/3})=y_{i}
, as follows. The law
\tilde{\mathbb}{P}^{[a,b],\bm{x},\bm{y}}_{N}
of
\tilde{\bm{L}}^{N}
is specified via its Radon-Nikodym derivative with respect to the law
\mathbb{P}^{k,[a,b],\bm{x},\bm{y}}_{\mathrm{free},N}
of
k
independent Bernoulli random walk bridges on
\llbracket aN^{2/3},bN^{2/3}\rrbracket
, the
j
th
from
(aN^{2/3},x_{j})
to
(bN^{2/3},y_{j})
for each
j
: for
\bm{\gamma}:\llbracket 1,k\rrbracket\times\llbracket aN^{2/3},bN^{2/3}\rrbracket\to\mathbb{Z}
a collection of
k
Bernoulli paths with endpoints
(aN^{2/3},x_{j})
and
(bN^{2/3},y_{j})
for
j=1,\ldots,k
,

(11.2)

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}\tilde{\mathbb}{P}^{[a,b],\bm{x},\bm{y}}_{N}}{\mathrm{d}\mathbb{P}^{k,[a,b],\bm{x},\bm{y}}_{\mathrm{free},N}}(\bm{\gamma})\propto\prod_{i=1}^{k}\prod_{x\in I_{N,T}}\left(1-q^{\Delta_{i}(x)}\mathbbm{1}_{\Delta_{i}(x)=\Delta_{i}(x-1)-1}\right)

where
I_{N,T}=\llbracket U_{1}N^{2/3},U_{2}N^{2/3}\rrbracket\setminus\llbracket-TN^{2/3},TN^{2/3}\rrbracket
and
\Delta_{i}(x)=\gamma_{i}(x)-\gamma_{i+1}(x)
with
\gamma_{k+1}=L^{N}_{k+1}
by convention. In words,
\tilde{\mathbb}{P}^{[a,b],\bm{x},\bm{y}}_{N}
is the usual Hall-Littlewood Gibbs measure but where the interaction is absent on
\llbracket-TN^{2/3},TN^{2/3}\rrbracket
.

Writing in the just introduced notation, the definition (
6.5
) reads

(11.3)

\displaystyle Z^{k,[-T,T],\bm{x},\bm{y},g}_{N}=\mathbb{E}^{k,[-T,T],\bm{x},\bm{y}}_{\mathrm{free},N}[W(\bm{B},g)]

(recall
W
from (
3.9
)), where, under
\mathbb{E}^{k,[-T,T],\bm{x},\bm{y}}_{\mathrm{free}}
,
\bm{B}=(B_{1},\ldots,B_{k})
is a collection of
k
independent Bernoulli random walk bridges, with
B_{i}
from
(-TN^{2/3},x_{i})
to
(TN^{2/3},y_{i})
.

Lemma 11.1
.

Suppose
g:[aN^{2/3},bN^{2/3}]\to\mathbb{R}
satisfies (with
\overline{g}(x):=g(x)-px
)

(11.4)

\begin{split}\overline{g}(x)&\leq\overline{g}(aN^{2/3})+(x-aN^{2/3})PN^{-1/3}\quad\text{for}\quad xN^{-2/3}\in[a,a+1]\\
\overline{g}(x)&\leq\overline{g}(bN^{2/3})+(bN^{2/3}-x)PN^{-1/3}\quad\text{for}\quad xN^{-2/3}\in[b-1,b],\end{split}

Suppose also that
M
is such that
\smash{\sup_{xN^{-2/3}\in[a,b]}|g(x)|\leq MN^{1/3}}
, and that there exists
\eta>0
such that
x_{i}>x_{i+1}+\eta\smash{N^{1/3}}
and
y_{i}>y_{i+1}+\eta\smash{N^{1/3}}
for each
i=1,\ldots,k
, with
x_{k+1}=g(a\smash{N^{2/3}})
and
y_{k+1}=g(bN^{2/3})
. Then there exist
\delta>0
,
\varepsilon>0
, and
N_{0}
, all depending on
k,M,T,\eta
, such that, for all
N\geq N_{0}
,

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathbb}{P}^{[a,b],\bm{x},\bm{y}}_{N}\left(Z^{k,[-T,T],\tilde{\bm{L}}^{N}(-TN^{2/3}),\tilde{\bm{L}}^{N}(TN^{2/3}),g}_{N}>\delta\right)>\varepsilon,

where
\tilde{\bm{L}}^{N}(TN^{2/3})
and
\tilde{\bm{L}}^{N}(-TN^{2/3})
in the superscript respectively indicate
(\tilde{L}^{N}_{i}(TN^{2/3}))_{i=1}^{k}
and
(\tilde{L}^{N}_{i}(-TN^{2/3}))_{i=1}^{k}
.

Proof.

We adopt the shorthand
Z^{k,T,g}_{N}=Z^{k,[-T,T],\tilde{\bm{L}}^{N}(-TN^{2/3}),\tilde{\bm{L}}^{N}(TN^{2/3}),g}_{N}
.
With some positive probability
2\varepsilon>0
(independent of
N
) it holds that
k
independent Bernoulli random walk bridges
\bm{B}=(B_{1},\ldots,B_{k})
starting from
(aN^{2/3},\bm{x})
and going to
(bN^{2/3},\bm{y})
reach points at
(a+1)N^{2/3}
and
(b-1)N^{2/3}
which are all above
2MN^{1/3}
and are separated by at least
N^{1/3}
; reach points at
\pm TN^{2/3}
which have the same properties; and do so while staying separated (from each other as well as from
g
) by at least
\frac{1}{2}\eta N^{1/3}
on
[aN^{2/3},-TN^{2/3}]
and
[TN^{2/3},bN^{2/3}]
; this uses the condition (
11.4
) controlling
g
and the initial separation of
\eta N^{1/3}
between the endpoints of
\bm{B}
. Call this event
\mathsf{Corr}
(for corridors).

On
\mathsf{Corr}
, it holds that the righthand side of (
11.5
) is lower bounded by
\frac{1}{2}
for all large enough
N
by lower bounding it as in the proof of Lemma
9.1
; and the implicit normalization factor in (
11.5
) is trivially upper bounded by
1
. Thus with
\smash{\tilde{\mathbb}{P}^{[a,b],\bm{x},\bm{y}}_{N}}
-probability at least
\varepsilon
, we have that the entries of
\smash{\tilde{\bm{L}}^{N}(-TN^{2/3})}
,
\smash{\tilde{\bm{L}}^{N}(TN^{2/3})}
are separated by at least
N^{1/3}
and are all above
2MN^{1/3}
.

We claim that there exists
\delta>0
such that, on the event that the entries of
\tilde{\bm{L}}^{N}(\pm TN^{2/3})
have the just mentioned properties,
\smash{Z^{k,T,g}_{N}}>\delta
. Indeed, with some positive probability
2\delta
,
k
independent Bernoulli random walk bridges from
(-TN^{2/3},\tilde{\bm{L}}^{N}(-TN^{2/3}))
to
(TN^{2/3},\tilde{\bm{L}}^{N}(TN^{2/3}))
will maintain separation at least
\frac{1}{2}N^{1/3}
throughout
[-TN^{2/3},TN^{2/3}]
while staying above
MN^{1/3}
. On this event, the weight function
W
is again lower bounded by
\smash{\frac{1}{2}}
for all large enough
N
by Lemma
9.1
. This completes the proof.
∎

Proof of Proposition
6.12
.

We adopt the shorthand
Z^{k,T}_{N}=Z^{k,[-T,T],\bm{L}^{N}(-TN^{2/3}),\bm{L}^{N}(TN^{2/3}),L^{N}_{k+1}}_{N}
.
By Theorem
6.10
(lower tail control) and Proposition
10.2
(upper tail control), there exists
M=M(\varepsilon,k,T)
such that
\mathbb{P}(\mathsf{RangeCtrl})\geq 1-\frac{1}{4}\varepsilon
, where

\mathsf{RangeCtrl}=\left\{\sup_{xN^{-2/3}\in[-2T,2T]}|L^{N}_{k+1}(x)|\leq MN^{1/3}\right\}.

Let
U_{1}
and
U_{2}
be as in (
11.1
). On
\mathsf{RangeCtrl}
, by Lemma
10.1
, there exists
P=P(\varepsilon,k,T,M)=P(\varepsilon,k,T)
such that
U_{1}\neq-2T
and
U_{2}\neq 2T
, i.e., (
11.1
) holds. Next, by Theorem
9.2
, let
\eta=\eta(\varepsilon,k,T)>0
be such that
\mathbb{P}(\mathsf{Sep}_{k}^{N}(\eta,\{U_{1},U_{2}\},\bm{L}^{N}))>1-\frac{1}{4}\varepsilon
. Let
\mathsf{Fav}=\mathsf{RangeCtrl}\cap\mathsf{Sep}_{k}^{N}(\eta,\{U_{1},U_{2}\},\bm{L}^{N})
, so that
\mathbb{P}(\mathsf{Fav}^{c})\leq\frac{1}{2}\varepsilon
.

Let
\mathcal{F}=\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{ext}}(k,\llbracket U_{1}N^{2/3},U_{2}N^{2/3}\rrbracket,\bm{L}^{N})
, and note that
\llbracket U_{1}N^{2/3},U_{2}N^{2/3}\rrbracket
forms a stopping domain with respect to
(L^{N}_{1},\ldots,L^{N}_{k})
(Definition
6.1
), since
U_{1},U_{2}
are measurable with respect to
L^{N}_{k+1}
. Conditionally on
\mathcal{F}
, let
\mathbb{P}_{N}
be the law of
\smash{\bm{L}^{N}|_{\llbracket 1,k\rrbracket\times\llbracket U_{1}N^{2/3},U_{2}N^{2/3}\rrbracket}}
and adopt the shorthand
\smash{\tilde{\mathbb}{P}_{N}}
for the law defined by (
11.2
). Let
\smash{\tilde{\bm{L}}^{N}\sim\tilde{\mathbb}{P}_{N}}
. Again conditionally on
\mathcal{F}
, define
\tilde{\bm{L}}^{N,\mathrm{res}}
and
\bm{L}^{N,\mathrm{res}}
(“res” short for restricted) by

\tilde{L}^{N,\mathrm{res}}_{i}:=\tilde{L}^{N}_{i}|_{\llbracket U_{1}N^{2/3},U_{2}N^{2/3}\rrbracket\setminus\llbracket-TN^{2/3},TN^{2/3}\rrbracket}

and
L^{N,\mathrm{res}}_{i}:=L^{N}_{i}|_{\llbracket U_{1}N^{2/3},U_{2}N^{2/3}\rrbracket\setminus\llbracket-TN^{2/3},TN^{2/3}\rrbracket}
for
i\in\llbracket 1,k\rrbracket
, and let
\tilde{\mathbb}{P}^{\mathrm{res}}_{N}
and
\mathbb{P}^{\mathrm{res}}_{N}
, respectively, be their laws. Note that, for any collection
\bm{\gamma}:\llbracket 1,k\rrbracket\times\llbracket U_{1}N^{2/3},U_{2}N^{2/3}\rrbracket\setminus\llbracket-TN^{2/3},TN^{2/3}\rrbracket\to\mathbb{Z}
of Bernoulli paths,

(11.5)

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}\mathbb{P}^{\mathrm{res}}_{N}}{\mathrm{d}\tilde{\mathbb}{P}^{\mathrm{res}}_{N}}(\bm{\gamma})=(\tilde{Z}^{k,T}_{N})^{-1}Z^{k,T}_{N}(\bm{\gamma}),

where
Z^{k,T}_{N}(\bm{\gamma}):=Z^{k,[-T,T],\bm{\gamma}(-TN^{2/3}),\bm{\gamma}(TN^{2/3}),L^{N}_{k+1}}_{N}
as in (
11.3
) and
\tilde{Z}^{k,T}_{N}:=\tilde{\mathbb}{E}^{\mathrm{res}}_{N}[Z^{k,T}_{N}(\bm{\gamma})]
. Since
Z^{k,T}_{N}(\bm{\gamma})
is a function of
\bm{\gamma}(\pm TN^{2/3})
(
L^{N}_{k+1}
is conditioned on), which have the same joint distribution when
\bm{\gamma}
is distributed as
\smash{\tilde{\mathbb}{P}^{\mathrm{res}}_{N}}
or
\smash{\tilde{\mathbb}{P}_{N}}
, it holds that
\tilde{Z}^{k,T}_{N}=\tilde{\mathbb}{E}_{N}[Z^{k,T}_{N}(\bm{\gamma})]
.
Similarly,
\smash{Z^{k,T}_{N}=Z^{k,T}_{N}(\bm{L}^{N})}
has the same distribution under
\mathbb{P}_{N}
and
\mathbb{P}^{\mathrm{res}}_{N}
. Thus, since
\mathsf{Fav}
is
\mathcal{F}
-measurable,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(Z^{k,T}_{N}<\delta\right)\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbb P_{\mathcal{F}}\left(Z^{k,T}_{N}<\delta\right)\mathbbm{1}_{\mathsf{Fav}}\right]+\mathbb{P}\left(\mathsf{Fav}^{c}\right)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbb{P}^{\mathrm{res}}_{N}\left(Z^{k,T}_{N}<\delta\right)\mathbbm{1}_{\mathsf{Fav}}\right]+\tfrac{1}{2}\varepsilon.

Now by (
11.5
), the previous display equals

(11.6)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[(\tilde{Z}^{k,T}_{N})^{-1}\tilde{\mathbb}{E}^{\mathrm{res}}_{N}\left[\mathbbm{1}_{Z^{k,T}_{N}<\delta}Z^{k,T}_{N}\right]\mathbbm{1}_{\mathsf{Fav}}\right]+\tfrac{1}{2}\varepsilon.

On
\mathsf{Fav}
,
(L^{N}_{i}(\pm TN^{2/3}))_{i=1}^{k}
and
L^{N}_{k+1}
satisfy the conditions in Lemma
11.1
. So we have that, for some
\delta^{\prime},\varepsilon^{\prime}>0
(the
\delta
,
\varepsilon
provided by Lemma
11.1
),

\displaystyle\tilde{Z}^{k,T}_{N}=\tilde{\mathbb}{E}[Z^{k,T}_{N}]\geq\rho:=\delta^{\prime}\varepsilon^{\prime},

This yields that (
11.6
) is upper bounded by

\displaystyle\rho^{-1}\cdot\mathbb{E}\left[\tilde{\mathbb}{E}^{\mathrm{res}}_{N}\left[\mathbbm{1}_{Z^{k,T}_{N}<\delta}Z^{k,T}_{N}\right]\right]+\tfrac{1}{2}\varepsilon\leq\delta\rho^{-1}+\tfrac{1}{2}\varepsilon.

Picking
\delta=\frac{1}{2}\varepsilon\rho
completes the proof.
∎

12.
Brownian Gibbs property in the limit

In this section we prove that all weak limit points of
\bm{\mathfrak{L}}^{N}
as defined in (
3.13
) possess the Brownian Gibbs property, thus establishing Proposition
6.5
. The first step is to compare the partition function associated to the Hall-Littlewood Gibbs property with the probability of separation (its analog for the Brownian Gibbs property); we do this in the next section. Then we will give the proof of Proposition
6.5
in Section
12.2
. As in the last few sections, we assume, without explicitly stating it again, that
\bm{L}^{N}
satisfies Assumptions
1
and
2
.

12.1.
Comparing the partition function and the separation probability

To emphasize the separation required with the lower boundary condition, in this section we extend the notation (
9.1
) as follows: for a set
A\subseteq\mathbb{R}
, an integer
k\in\mathbb{N}
,
\Lambda\subseteq\mathbb{N}
a (possibly infinite) interval with
\llbracket 1,k\rrbracket\subseteq\Lambda
, a process
\bm{X}:\Lambda\times\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{R}
, a process
Y:\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{R}
, and
\delta>0
, we define the event

(12.1)

\begin{split}\mathsf{Sep}^{N}_{k}(\delta,A,\bm{X},Y)&=\left\{\min_{i=1,\ldots,k-1}\inf_{xN^{-2/3}\in A}\bigl(X_{i}(x)-X_{i+1}(x)\bigr)\geq\delta N^{1/3}\right\}\\
&\qquad\cap\left\{\inf_{xN^{-2/3}\in A}\bigl(X_{k}(x)-Y(x)\bigr)\geq\delta N^{1/3}\right\},\end{split}

i.e., in the notation (
9.1
), if we define
\tilde{\bm{X}}:\llbracket 1,k+1\rrbracket\times\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{R}
by
\tilde{X}_{i}(\bm{\cdot})=X_{i}(\bm{\cdot})
for
i=1,\ldots,k
and
\tilde{X}_{k+1}(\bm{\cdot})=Y(\bm{\cdot})
, then
\mathsf{Sep}^{N}_{k}(\delta,A,\bm{X},Y)=\mathsf{Sep}^{N}_{k}(\delta,A,\tilde{\bm{X}})
. At times we will drop the set
A
from the notation, but this will be explicitly stated.

For the next statement, recall
W
from Definition
3.15
.

Lemma 12.1
.

Fix
a>b
and
k\in\mathbb{N}
. Let
\mathcal{F}=\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{ext}}(k,\llbracket aN^{2/3},bN^{2/3}\rrbracket,\bm{L}^{N})
and
\bm{B}=(B_{1},\ldots,B_{k})
be a collection of
k
independent Bernoulli random walk bridges, with
B_{i}
from
(aN^{2/3},L^{N}_{i}(aN^{2/3}))
to
(bN^{2/3},L^{N}_{i}(bN^{2/3}))
. For any
\varepsilon>0
, there exist
N_{0}=N_{0}(\varepsilon,k,b-a)
and
\delta=\delta(\varepsilon,k,b-a)>0
such that, for all
N\geq N_{0}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{\mathbb E_{\mathcal{F}}[W(\bm{B},L^{N}_{k+1},\llbracket aN^{2/3},bN^{2/3}\rrbracket)]}{\mathbb P_{\mathcal{F}}(\mathsf{Sep}^{N}_{k}(\delta,[a,b],\bm{B},L^{N}_{k+1}))}\in[1-\varepsilon,1+\varepsilon]\right)\geq 1-\varepsilon.

Proof.

Adopt the shorthand
\mathsf{Sep}^{N}_{k}(\delta,\bm{B},L^{N}_{k+1})=\mathsf{Sep}^{N}_{k}(\delta,[a,b],\bm{B},L^{N}_{k+1})
and drop the interval
\llbracket aN^{2/3},bN^{2/3}\rrbracket
from the
W
notation. We first pick
\delta>0
such that, by Theorem
9.2
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\mathsf{Sep}^{N}_{k}(\delta,\bm{L}^{N},L^{N}_{k+1}))\geq 1-\varepsilon^{2}.

Since the lefthand side of this inequality equals
\mathbb{E}[\mathbb P_{\mathcal{F}}(\mathsf{Sep}^{N}_{k}(\delta,\bm{L}^{N},L^{N}_{k+1}))]
, and the conditional probability is a random variable taking values in
[0,1]
, it follows that, with probability at least
1-\varepsilon
,

\displaystyle\mathbb P_{\mathcal{F}}(\mathsf{Sep}^{N}_{k}(\delta,\bm{L}^{N},L^{N}_{k+1}))\geq 1-\varepsilon.

We work on the event that the previous inequality holds. We have the following string of inequalities, using
W(\bm{B},L^{N}_{k+1})\leq 1
in the first inequality, the Hall-Littlewood Gibbs property to go from the second to the third line, and the previous display for the inequality in the last line:

\displaystyle\mathbb P_{\mathcal{F}}(\mathsf{Sep}^{N}_{k}(\delta,\bm{B},L^{N}_{k+1}))

\displaystyle\geq\mathbb E_{\mathcal{F}}[\mathbbm{1}_{\mathsf{Sep}^{N}_{k}(\delta,\bm{B},L^{N}_{k+1})}W(\bm{B},L^{N}_{k+1})]

\displaystyle=\frac{\mathbb E_{\mathcal{F}}[\mathbbm{1}_{\mathsf{Sep}^{N}_{k}(\delta,\bm{B},L^{N}_{k+1})}W(\bm{B},L^{N}_{k+1})]}{\mathbb E_{\mathcal{F}}[W(\bm{B},L^{N}_{k+1})]}\cdot\mathbb E_{\mathcal{F}}[W(\bm{B},L^{N}_{k+1})]

\displaystyle=\mathbb P_{\mathcal{F}}(\mathsf{Sep}^{N}_{k}(\delta,\bm{L}^{N},L^{N}_{k+1}))\cdot\mathbb E_{\mathcal{F}}[W(\bm{B},L^{N}_{k+1})]\geq(1-\varepsilon)\mathbb E_{\mathcal{F}}[W(\bm{B},L^{N}_{k+1})].

It also trivially holds that
\mathbb E_{\mathcal{F}}[W(\bm{B},L^{N}_{k+1})]\geq\mathbb E_{\mathcal{F}}[W(\bm{B},\smash{L^{N}_{k+1}})\mathbbm{1}_{\mathsf{Sep}^{N}_{k}(\delta,\bm{B},\smash{L^{N}_{k+1}})}]
and that, on the event
\smash{\mathsf{Sep}^{N}_{k}}(\delta,\bm{B},\smash{L^{N}_{k+1}})
and by Lemma
9.1
,
W(\bm{B},\smash{L^{N}_{k+1}})\geq 1-\varepsilon
for all large enough
N
. This yields that, for such
N
,

\displaystyle\mathbb E_{\mathcal{F}}[W(\bm{B},L^{N}_{k+1})]\geq(1-\varepsilon)\mathbb P_{\mathcal{F}}(\mathsf{Sep}^{N}_{k}(\delta,\bm{B},L^{N}_{k+1})).

This completes the proof after relabeling
\varepsilon
.
∎

Corollary 12.2
.

Fix
\varepsilon\in(0,1)
,
k\in\mathbb{N}
,
M>0
, and
a<b
. Let
\mathcal{F}=\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{ext}}(k,[aN^{2/3},bN^{2/3}]
,
\bm{L}^{N})
and
\bm{B}=(B_{1},\ldots,B_{k})
be a collection of
k
independent Bernoulli random walk bridges with
B_{i}
from
(aN^{2/3},L^{N}_{i}(aN^{2/3}))
to
(bN^{2/3},L^{N}_{i}(bN^{2/3}))
. There exist
N_{0}=N_{0}(\varepsilon,k,b-a,M)
and
\delta=\delta(\varepsilon,k,b-a,M)>0
such that, for any
N\geq N_{0}
, the following holds with probability at least
1-\varepsilon
. For any bounded measurable function
F:\mathcal{C}(\llbracket 1,k\rrbracket\times[aN^{2/3},bN^{2/3}],\mathbb{R})\to\mathbb{R}
such that
\sup|F|\leq M
,

\displaystyle\left|\frac{\mathbb E_{\mathcal{F}}\left[F(\bm{B})W(\bm{B},L^{N}_{k+1},\llbracket aN^{2/3},bN^{2/3}\rrbracket)\right]}{\mathbb E_{\mathcal{F}}[W(\bm{B},L^{N}_{k+1},\llbracket aN^{2/3},bN^{2/3}\rrbracket)]}-\mathbb E_{\mathcal{F}}\left[F(\bm{B})\mid\mathsf{Sep}^{N}_{k}(\delta,[a,b],\bm{B},L^{N}_{k+1})\right]\right|\leq\varepsilon.

Proof.

We again adopt the shorthand
\mathsf{Sep}^{N}_{k}(\delta,\bm{B},L^{N}_{k+1})=\mathsf{Sep}^{N}_{k}(\delta,[a,b],\bm{B},L^{N}_{k+1})
and drop the interval
\llbracket aN^{2/3},bN^{2/3}\rrbracket
from the
W
notation. We may assume that
F\geq 0
as we have assumed
F
is bounded and adding a constant to
F
does not change the lefthand side of the inequality we are proving.
Let
\varepsilon^{\prime}=\varepsilon M^{-1}
. Pick
\delta>0
from Lemma
12.1
with
\varepsilon
there equal to
\varepsilon^{\prime}
, and let
N_{0}=N_{0}(\varepsilon,k,b-a,M)
be such that
W(\bm{B},L^{N}_{k+1})\geq 1-\varepsilon^{\prime}
on
\mathsf{Sep}^{N}_{k}(\delta,\bm{B},L^{N}_{k+1})
for
N>N_{0}
(by Lemma
9.1
). Then

\displaystyle\frac{\mathbb E_{\mathcal{F}}\left[F(\bm{B})W(\bm{B},L^{N}_{k+1})\right]}{\mathbb E_{\mathcal{F}}[W(\bm{B},L^{N}_{k+1})]}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{\mathbb E_{\mathcal{F}}\left[F(\bm{B})\mathbbm{1}_{\mathsf{Sep}^{N}_{k}(\delta,\bm{B},L^{N}_{k+1})}W(\bm{B},L^{N}_{k+1})\right]}{\mathbb E_{\mathcal{F}}[W(\bm{B},L^{N}_{k+1})]}

\displaystyle\geq(1-\varepsilon^{\prime})\frac{\mathbb E_{\mathcal{F}}\left[F(\bm{B})\mathbbm{1}_{\mathsf{Sep}^{N}_{k}(\delta,\bm{B},L^{N}_{k+1})}\right]}{\mathbb E_{\mathcal{F}}[W(\bm{B},L^{N}_{k+1})]}.

Now invoking Lemma
12.1
and our choice of
\delta
, with probability at least
1-\varepsilon
, the previous display is lower bounded by

\displaystyle\frac{1-\varepsilon^{\prime}}{1+\varepsilon^{\prime}}\cdot\frac{\mathbb E_{\mathcal{F}}\left[F(\bm{B})\mathbbm{1}_{\mathsf{Sep}^{N}_{k}(\delta,\bm{B},L^{N}_{k+1})}\right]}{\mathbb P_{\mathcal{F}}(\mathsf{Sep}^{N}_{k}(\delta,\bm{B},L^{N}_{k+1}))}=\frac{1-\varepsilon^{\prime}}{1+\varepsilon^{\prime}}\cdot\mathbb E_{\mathcal{F}}[F(\bm{B})\mid\mathsf{Sep}^{N}_{k}(\delta,\bm{B},L^{N}_{k+1})].

Rearranging this gives one side of the claimed inequality after relabeling
\varepsilon
, since
F
is bounded by
M
, and the other is obtained by replacing
F
with
M-F
, and rearranging appropriately again.
∎

12.2.
Brownian Gibbs property in the limit

Throughout this section, we recall the definition of the Brownian Gibbs property from Definition
6.4
.

Lemma 12.3
.

For each
N\in\mathbb{N}
, let
B^{N}
be a Bernoulli random walk bridge from
(0,0)
to
(N,z_{N})
, and suppose
z_{N}/N\to p\in(-1,0)
as
N\to\infty
. Then

\displaystyle N^{-1/2}(B^{N}(Nt)-z_{N}t)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{\to}}B^{\mathrm{Br}}(t)

as
N\to\infty
as processes on
\mathcal{C}([0,1],\mathbb{R})
with the topology of uniform convergence, where
B^{\mathrm{Br}}
is a Brownian bridge on
[0,1]
with zero endpoints and variance
p(1-p)
.

Proof.

This follows from the KMT coupling Lemma
6.14
(with the
p
there equal to
z_{N}/N
here).
∎

Proof of Proposition
6.5
.

It is sufficient to prove the resampling property when resampling the top
k
curves for any
k\in\mathbb{N}
, i.e, prove Definition
6.4
holds with
j=1
, as the conditional distribution in the case of
j>1
is obtained from the
j=1
case along with the fact that non-intersecting Brownian bridges have the Brownian Gibbs property themselves
[
DM21
, Lemma 2.13]
(this is stated in the case of no lower boundary condition, but the proof also applies if there is one). More precisely, given an interval
[a,b]
and knowledge that the Brownian Gibbs property holds for a line ensemble
\bm{\mathfrak{L}}
when
j=1
(i.e., when conditioned on
\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{ext}}(k,[a,b],\bm{\mathfrak{L}})
), to obtain the same for
j>1
, one further conditions on
\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{ext}}(\llbracket j,k\rrbracket,[a,b],\bm{\mathfrak{L}})
and makes use of
[
DM21
, Lemma 2.13]
along with the fact that iterative conditioning is commutative
[
Kal21
, Theorem 8.15]
.

Now we turn to prove the
j=1
case. For
k\in\mathbb{N}
and
a<b
, let
\bm{\mathfrak{B}}:\llbracket 1,k\rrbracket\times[a,b]\to\mathbb{R}
with
\bm{\mathfrak{B}}=(\mathfrak{B}_{1},\ldots,\mathfrak{B}_{k})
be a collection of
k
rate two independent Brownian bridges, with
\mathfrak{B}_{i}(x)=\mathfrak{L}_{i}(x)
for
i\in\llbracket 1,k\rrbracket
and
x\in\{a,b\}
. Similar to (
12.1
), we extend the definition (
9.17
) to emphasize the lower boundary condition: for an interval
I
, define

\displaystyle\mathsf{NonInt}_{k}(I,\bm{\mathfrak{B}},\mathfrak{L}_{k+1}):=\Bigl\{\mathfrak{B}_{1}(x)>\mathfrak{B}_{2}(x)>\ldots>\mathfrak{B}_{k}(x)>\mathfrak{L}_{k+1}(x)\text{ for all }x\in I\Bigr\}.

In the rest of the proof we take
I=[a,b]
and omit it from the notation. Let
\mathcal{F}=\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{ext}}(k,[a,b],\bm{\mathfrak{L}})
. For any
k\in\mathbb{N}
,
a<b
, and
F:\mathcal{C}(\llbracket 1,k\rrbracket\times[a,b],\mathbb{R})\to\mathbb{R}
bounded and continuous, we have to show that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[F(\bm{\mathfrak{L}})\mid\mathcal{F}\right]=\mathbb E_{\mathcal{F}}[F(\bm{\mathfrak{B}})\mid\mathsf{NonInt}(\bm{\mathfrak{B}},\mathfrak{L}_{k+1})],

where
F(\bm{X})
is shorthand for
F(\bm{X}|_{\llbracket 1,k\rrbracket\times[a,b]})
.

By the definition of conditional expectations, and a monotone class argument, the previous display will be proven if we show that, for any
j\in\mathbb{N}
,
c<d
, and
G:\mathcal{C}(\llbracket 1,j\rrbracket\times[c,d]\setminus(\llbracket 1,k\rrbracket\times[a,b]),\mathbb{R})\to\mathbb{R}
bounded and continuous (with
G(\bm{X})
shorthand for
G(\bm{X}|_{\llbracket 1,j\rrbracket\times[c,d]\setminus(\llbracket 1,k\rrbracket\times[a,b])})
),

(12.2)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[F(\bm{\mathfrak{L}})\cdot G(\bm{\mathfrak{L}})\right]=\mathbb{E}\bigl[\mathbb E_{\mathcal{F}}[F(\bm{\mathfrak{B}})\mid\mathsf{NonInt}(\bm{\mathfrak{B}},\mathfrak{L}_{k+1})]\cdot G(\bm{\mathfrak{L}})\bigr].

By Skorokhod’s representation theorem and by the weak convergence of
\bm{\mathfrak{L}}^{N}
to
\bm{\mathfrak{L}}
, we may assume that we work on a probability space such that
\bm{\mathfrak{L}}^{N}\to\bm{\mathfrak{L}}
uniformly on compact sets almost surely. Then we obtain, with
\mathcal{F}^{N}:=\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{ext}}^{N}(k,\llbracket\beta^{-1}aN^{2/3},\beta^{-1}bN^{2/3}\rrbracket,\bm{L}^{N})
, by the dominated convergence theorem and the Hall-Littlewood Gibbs property, that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[F(\bm{\mathfrak{L}})\cdot G(\bm{\mathfrak{L}})\right]

\displaystyle=\lim_{N\to\infty}\mathbb{E}\left[F(\bm{\mathfrak{L}}^{N})\cdot G(\bm{\mathfrak{L}}^{N})\right]

\displaystyle=\lim_{N\to\infty}\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbb{E}[F(\bm{\mathfrak{L}}^{N})\mid\mathcal{F}^{N}]\cdot G(\bm{\mathfrak{L}}^{N})\right]

(12.3)

\displaystyle=\lim_{N\to\infty}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{F}^{N}}[F(\bm{\mathfrak{B}}^{N})W(\bm{B}^{N},L^{N}_{k+1})]}{\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{F}^{N}}[W(\bm{B}^{N},L^{N}_{k+1})]}\cdot G(\bm{\mathfrak{L}}^{N})\right],

the last line using the Hall-Littlewood Gibbs property and with
\bm{\mathfrak{B}}^{N}=(B^{N}_{1},\ldots,B^{N}_{k}):\llbracket 1,k\rrbracket\times[a,b]\to\mathbb{R}
given by (recall
p
from Assumption
2
,
\beta
and
\sigma
from (
3.12
), and the definition of
\bm{\mathfrak{L}}^{N}
in terms of
\bm{L}^{N}
from (
3.13
))
\bm{\mathfrak{B}}^{N}=T_{p,\lambda,N}(\bm{B}^{N})
, i.e.,

\mathfrak{B}^{N}_{i}(x)=\sigma^{-1}N^{-1/3}\left(B^{N}_{i}(\beta xN^{2/3})-p\beta xN^{2/3}\right)

with
{\bm{B}}^{N}=(B^{N}_{1},\ldots,B^{N}_{k})
a family of
k
independent Bernoulli random walk bridges, with
B^{N}_{i}
from
(\beta^{-1}aN^{2/3},L^{N}_{i}(\beta^{-1}aN^{2/3}))
to
(\beta^{-1}bN^{2/3},L^{N}_{i}(\beta^{-1}bN^{2/3}))
. Now, by Lemma
12.3
, it holds that
\bm{\mathfrak{B}}^{N}
converges weakly as
N\to\infty
to
\bm{\mathfrak{B}}=(\mathfrak{B}_{1},\ldots,\mathfrak{B}_{k})
, as above, a collection of
k
independent rate two Brownian bridges with
\mathfrak{B}_{i}
going from
(a,\mathfrak{L}_{i}(a))
to
(b,\mathfrak{L}_{i}(b))
; the fact that the rate of the Brownian bridges is
2
is a consequence of the relation (
3.12
) between
\beta
and
\sigma
in the definitions of
\bm{\mathfrak{L}}^{N}
and thus
\bm{\mathfrak{B}}^{N}
.

Recall the extended definition of
\mathsf{Sep}
from (
12.1
). Because it requires separation of order
N^{1/3}
, which should not be present for the limiting objects, we define an event
\mathsf{ScSep}
(scaled separation) for an interval
[a,b]\subset\mathbb{R}
, an integer
k\in\mathbb{N}
, a (possibly infinite) interval
\Lambda\subseteq\mathbb{N}
such that
\llbracket 1,k\rrbracket\subseteq\Lambda
, and processes
\bm{X}:\Lambda\times\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{R}
and
Y:\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{R}
, by

\displaystyle\mathsf{ScSep}_{k}(\delta,[a,b],\bm{X},Y)

\displaystyle:=\left\{\min_{i=1,\ldots,k-1}\inf_{x\in[a,b]}\left(X_{i}(x)-X_{i+1}(x)\right)\geq\delta\right\}

\displaystyle\qquad\cap\left\{\inf_{x\in[a,b]}\left(X_{k+1}(x)-Y(x)\right)\geq\delta\right\}.

Then observe that, by the definitions of
\bm{\mathfrak{B}}^{N}
and
\bm{\mathfrak{L}}^{N}
, it holds that

\displaystyle\mathsf{Sep}^{N}_{k}(\delta\sigma,[\beta^{-1}a,\beta^{-1}b],\bm{B}^{N},L^{N}_{k+1})=\mathsf{ScSep}_{k}(\delta,[a,b],\bm{\mathfrak{B}}^{N},\mathfrak{L}^{N}_{k+1}).

By Corollary
12.2
and the previous display, we obtain that there is a sequence
\varepsilon_{N,\delta}\downarrow\varepsilon_{\delta}
as
N\to\infty
, where
\varepsilon_{\delta}
is a sequence which goes to
0
as
\delta\downarrow 0
, such that (
12.3
) equals

\displaystyle\lim_{N\to\infty}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{F}^{N}}[F(\bm{\mathfrak{B}}^{N})\mid\mathsf{ScSep}_{k}(\delta,[a,b],\bm{\mathfrak{B}}^{N},\mathfrak{L}^{N}_{k+1})]+\varepsilon_{N,\delta}\right)\cdot G(\bm{\mathfrak{L}}^{N})\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\lim_{N\to\infty}\left(\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{F}^{N}}[F(\bm{B}^{N})\mid\mathsf{ScSep}_{k}(\delta,[a,b],\bm{\mathfrak{B}}^{N},\mathfrak{L}^{N}_{k+1})]+\varepsilon_{N,\delta}\right)\cdot\lim_{N\to\infty}G(\bm{\mathfrak{L}}^{N})\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[(\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{F}}[F(\bm{\mathfrak{B}})\mid\mathsf{ScSep}_{k}(\delta,[a,b],\bm{\mathfrak{B}},\mathfrak{L}_{k+1})]+\varepsilon_{\delta})\cdot G(\bm{\mathfrak{L}})\right];

the penultimate equality used the dominated convergence theorem since
F
and
G
are bounded and continuous, and the final equality used the Portmanteau theorem along with the fact that
\mathbb{P}(\mathsf{ScSep}_{k}(\delta,[a,b],\bm{\mathfrak{B}},\mathfrak{L}_{k+1}))

>0
(since
\mathfrak{L}_{k+1}
is a continuous function and with probability
1
there is strictly positive separation between the endpoints of
\mathfrak{B}_{i}
and
\mathfrak{B}_{i+1}
as well as between those of
\mathfrak{B}_{k}
and
\mathfrak{B}_{k+1}
, as follows from Theorem
9.2
) to also take the limit of the conditioning.

Finally taking
\delta\to 0
and using that
